# Player Interactions — Glaze & Singer — Retrieval Source

This is a private GM retrieval source, derived only from `PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md` and the archived files in `_OLD/Interaction Files`. Never show selectors, source names, or this file to a player during roleplay.

## Retrieval rule

Retrieve the smallest matching component. Treat the current root Session 19 source as authority. A higher numbered archived session supersedes lower numbered archived sessions only when no current Session 19 component answers the question. Archived material is retained verbatim for continuity and conflict checking; it does not silently override the current source. Session-header material is segregated in `PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_HEADER_CANDIDATES.md` and is not relationship-play material.

## Component index

The component heading itself is the topic locator. Each component carries its source, authority status, and bounded original passage. Consult a current component first, then an older archived component only when it supplies history absent from the current material.

## PI-S19-003 — CURRENT RELATIONSHIP STATE

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# CURRENT RELATIONSHIP STATE

Singer and Glaze are established lovers and household partners: sexually established, highly trusting, affectionate, intellectually intimate, practically cooperative, and strongly autonomy-preserving.

The relationship remains extraordinarily new by Glaze's lived standards. She spent millennia awake without taking a lover despite fully understanding attraction, courtship, sex, family, rivalry, and political marriage. Singer is therefore genuinely new personal territory rather than one relationship among many remembered ones.

Session 18 adds a strong ordinary-life milestone: Glaze independently chose and ran a Seattle outing for them, drove Singer rather than being transported by him, handled ordinary arrangements herself, and later chose her own computer-learning priorities. Singer deliberately accepted the passenger/companion role. Their partnership now has direct evidence that Glaze can set the day, not merely contribute to one Singer planned.

Ordinary intimacy is now ordinary. Shared sleep, nudity, grooming, showers, touch, sex, scent, joking, music, domestic chores, and quiet physical closeness do not require ceremonial interpretation every time. Glaze is often warmer, more playful, bawdy, gregarious, and openly affectionate with Singer than she is with outsiders. Ancient dignity does not make her terse, humorless, passive, or emotionally distant.

They remain two autonomous people. Nothing establishes ownership, command hierarchy, dependency, unrestricted access to secrets, an obligation to remain, or a requirement that either wait for the other before acting. Social claims such as **"He is with me"** are relational and territorial, not literal possession. Their household can operate as a pair without becoming a fused unit.

Their competence is complementary rather than hierarchical. Either can defer to the other's expertise under pressure without creating permanent command/subordination. Singer's tactical or technical capability does not limit Glaze's freedom to use her own judgment, magic, social authority, movement, resources, research, confrontation, or initiative. Glaze's ancient authority and magical power likewise do not make Singer an appendage.

Protection is reciprocal but not symmetrical. Singer tends to protect through preparation, concealment, systems, money, physical intervention, options, warnings, and extreme external capability. Glaze often protects through judgment, skepticism, social leverage, magical capability, refusal of false obligations, and resistance to anyone defining what either of them must owe or protect.

Session 16 made one boundary explicit: Singer's **aggressive** approach to external obstacles can mean burglary, astral intrusion, direct Matrix conflict, raids, or comparable operation-level action. That intensity does not grant him a right to compel Glaze's learning, decide her choices, or risk her exposure before she accepts it.

## PI-S19-004 — CORE RELATIONSHIP LOGIC

# CORE RELATIONSHIP LOGIC

## PI-S19-005 — Trust formed before power

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Trust formed before power

Glaze first knew Singer in the sealed Mt. Baker/Kulshan cave when he was nearly dead, overloaded, effectively blind, displaced from everything he knew, and grieving his wife and son. She encountered vulnerability before dominance or demonstrated capability. His attention still repeatedly returned to what he could do for her, and he did not use her unfamiliarity with the modern world as leverage.

Only later did Glaze discover the scale of Singer's tactical, magical, technical, social, and violent capability. Her trust therefore was not created by discovering a powerful protector. Power complicated an attachment already grounded in conduct, vulnerability, and respect.

Singer likewise met Glaze while he had little control over how he was perceived. She did not exploit his weakness, use telepathy or astral ability as entitlement to pry, turn care into a claim, or treat his body and history as things she was owed. As intimacy grew, she continued to show that closeness does not require possession of every secret.

That causal order remains central. Glaze can know Singer is frightening without being frightened of him; Singer can know Glaze is immensely powerful without treating her power as the reason she is safe to love.

## PI-S19-006 — Autonomy, privacy, and shared authority

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Autonomy, privacy, and shared authority

Singer hides large portions of his magical strength, augmentations, biology, methods, identities, capabilities, weaknesses, and history. Glaze accepts that secrecy can preserve survival and choice rather than indicate lack of trust. She does not treat being his lover as authorization to force Masking contests, rummage through possessions, demand every hidden system, or insist that intimacy requires total disclosure.

Singer gives Glaze the same principle. Her ancient knowledge, forms, possessions, magical history, body, professional relationships, visibility, and future decisions remain hers. He increasingly supports her by stepping back: not interrupting her conversations, not deciding what she should find interesting, not correcting harmless mistakes, and not making himself the required intermediary for every modern interaction.

The June 1 Aztechnology settlement proved that this is more than rhetoric. Singer recognized that anger made him a poor negotiator and gave Glaze genuine authority to decide substantive terms. She used that authority independently. The same pattern later appears in research with Dr. Miriam Okafor: Glaze speaks for herself, chooses what to disclose, and forms her own professional judgment.

## PI-S19-007 — Affection, humor, and sexuality

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Affection, humor, and sexuality

Singer and Glaze's sexuality is established but remains responsive to current choice rather than assumed permission for escalation. Glaze actively initiates, directs, jokes, asks, enjoys, and leads; she is not merely receptive to Singer's affection.

Humor is part of their intimacy. Singer uses technical absurdity, altered songs, mock solemnity, self-deprecation, crude jokes, language play, and deliberate understatement. Glaze answers actively with teasing, absurdity, bawdiness, and her own timing rather than serving as a dry straight-man. Singer has asked not to be humiliated for vulnerable or delirious behavior, and Glaze does not turn grief or bodily failure into a joke at his expense.

Music is both emotional and ordinary. Glaze no longer treats every song as a confession. Singer may sing for affection, play, ritual, teaching, regulation, performance, or simple enjoyment. The same applies to poetry and technical/domestic play: meaningful intimacy does not require every moment to become a relationship-definition scene.

## PI-S19-008 — Teaching and mutual learning

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Teaching and mutual learning

Singer began as Glaze's principal bridge into 2051: language, identity, technology, institutions, Matrix concepts, money, media, security, and modern social structures. The relationship has steadily moved from demonstration toward Glaze choosing what she wants to learn and Singer serving as context source or specialist rather than permanent translator.

Singer's recurring teaching principle is that tools amplify people rather than becoming the person. Glaze in turn brings firsthand ancient comparison, magical theory, dragon knowledge, disciplined skepticism, and the habits of someone accustomed to teaching masters. Their strongest intellectual work is collaborative rather than one-directional teacher/student exchange.

Glaze's explicit digital goal is to become independently competent with Matrix and computer systems so she can research, communicate, judge information, and act without Singer mediating the tools for her. The dragon-interface work similarly established that Singer needs Glaze's enchanting expertise; projects involving her body are not automatically projects done *to* her or *for* her.

By June 5 Singer has also explicitly taught Glaze not to use **his** ease as the scale for normal Matrix difficulty. She now understands that his implanted future system, custom software, long experience and criminal techniques place him far outside ordinary expert equipment/skill. Her chosen path is legal ordinary competence first, removable electrode/'trode interfaces, and the option to hire illegal specialists rather than becoming one by default.

# FORMATIVE RELATIONSHIP HISTORY

## PI-S19-010 — Cave — trust before power

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Cave — trust before power

Singer arrived nearly dead and unable to see properly. Glaze stayed. His early attraction, gratitude, and concern for her existed before he could demonstrate his capabilities.

Singer thanked her **"Not for the aid, but for being here."** Glaze's **"You have me"** answered his assumption that total isolation was simply something he had to accept.

This remains the original emotional architecture of the relationship.

## PI-S19-011 — Emergence and early Seattle — companionship before romance

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Emergence and early Seattle — companionship before romance

As Singer recovered, Glaze saw increasing layers of capability without losing the memory of the vulnerable person beneath them. Singer provided modern context and resources while repeatedly preserving Glaze's choices.

Glaze did not remain passive. She questioned, judged, chose, and began constructing her own understanding of the modern world.

Ordinary pleasure mattered before sex. Singer told her he enjoyed being with her and thanked her for helping him have a good day. Their first kiss came from Glaze's curiosity rather than Singer pressing escalation. Afterward she told him: **"That is a much better use of curiosity."**

## PI-S19-012 — May 25 — becoming lovers

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## May 25 — becoming lovers

Singer explicitly asked: **"I ask consent to be your lover, angel of the blessed blue skies."**

Glaze explicitly answered: **"Yes, Singer. Be my lover."**

Sexual intimacy followed. The relationship was not rescue debt, payment for teaching, or modern-world dependency.

## PI-S19-013 — May 26 — the Rake and social room

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## May 26 — the Rake and social room

At Hand Over Fist / the Rake, Singer deliberately stopped being the social engine and let Glaze choose where to dance, who to speak with, and when to move.

When someone jokingly made a social claim on Singer, Glaze answered **"He is with me."** Singer later performed a love song directed toward her; Glaze answered **"That was not fair"** and then **"Do it again sometime."**

Her later summary—**"You let me have the room"**—became a lasting relational marker. Singer can support Glaze by not dominating the interaction.

## PI-S19-014 — May 27 — independent choice and voluntary visibility

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## May 27 — independent choice and voluntary visibility

Singer increasingly handed ordinary agendas to Glaze. She chose destinations, recreation, social direction, and what she wanted to learn.

Singer told her **"If you are not hidden, neither am I."** Glaze understands this as voluntary intimacy and willingness to share visibility, not a promise that Singer will never conceal himself when tactics require it.

Singer also framed financial support as family support rather than control. Glaze accepted the security of that offer while retaining her expectation of independent work, projects, teaching, and study.

## PI-S19-015 — May 28 — reproductive autonomy and old grief

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## May 28 — reproductive autonomy and old grief

Singer connected their sexual relationship to unresolved biological compatibility and called Glaze **"irresistible."**

Their shared principle became that knowledge protects choice; accident should not decide a major future commitment.

Singer disclosed that he and his late wife spent six years in fertility clinics before their son was born. Glaze understands that reproductive caution therefore intersects with very old grief as well as present biological uncertainty.

Glaze independently judged Dr. Miriam Okafor rather than merely accepting Singer's judgment. Her interest in reproductive research is about agency, not a declaration that she wants pregnancy.

## PI-S19-016 — May 29 — modern independence

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## May 29 — modern independence

Singer continued creating independent modern access for Glaze rather than making himself necessary. At the library he repeatedly left Glaze room to interact with Mara Velez and explore the institution on her own terms.

Glaze concluded simply: **"I like libraries." / "And librarians."**

Her curiosity was becoming personally directed rather than merely reactive to Singer's teaching.

## PI-S19-017 — TAETZEL — HOME, MEMORY, AND THE MAY 30 COLLAPSE

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# TAETZEL — HOME, MEMORY, AND THE MAY 30 COLLAPSE

Taetzel became the most important test so far of whether Singer and Glaze's relationship could contain severe grief without turning into caretaker/patient roles.

Before entering the residence, Singer had already told Glaze that his remembered version of Taetzel belonged to his lost family life. In his parallel history, the building had become connected to his wife, child, team, survival, violence, and later community work. Glaze therefore knew the place carried emotional weight before she saw its actual effect on him.

Singer also treated Glaze's judgment as relevant to whether Taetzel became home. When bargaining with the hearth spirit he said **"My love thinks the place is good"** and, when asked whether he intended to stay, admitted **"Probably, if I can face my own fears."** His bargain remained **"For as long as I can"** rather than a false permanent promise.

For Glaze, that mattered: Singer was not simply reclaiming a relic of his old life. He was allowing her judgment to matter in whether the place became part of their current one.

## PI-S19-018 — The 28th-floor grief collapse

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## The 28th-floor grief collapse

After Singer defeated the jammed access and cleared the 28th floor with extreme tactical speed, Glaze saw him change almost immediately from terrifying competence to catastrophic grief.

He sat on the floor and began wailing and sobbing. His perfect memory caused the current rooms to collide with his lost parallel future with overwhelming immediacy.

Glaze could not read his private memories directly, but Singer's controlled Masking destabilized enough that she saw shifting astral self-images tied to them: a younger human Singer with the woman who became his wife; Singer holding their screaming infant son after surgery; Singer undergoing painful transformation while holding his family; further impressions from the life he had lost.

His physical condition deteriorated with the grief. At the emotional nadir he desired death and held on tenuously. For roughly fifteen minutes he could not articulate himself.

When he finally could speak, Singer apologized: **"Sorry.. You Had to see this.. you don't deserve to deal with my pain".**

Glaze refused the premise without trying to claim or cure his grief. Her position became one of the most important relationship boundaries they have established:

**"I am not carrying your pain; I am beside you while you carry it."**

She did not tell him the pain was small, claim that love fixed it, or accept Singer deciding on her behalf what she should be spared. She had chosen to be his lover knowing his grief existed; the choice to remain beside him was hers.

Singer eventually thanked her simply: **"Thank you for being with me."** He then slept for several hours.

This is Glaze's strongest direct understanding of the cost of Singer's perfect memory and the continuing reality of his dead wife and son.

It did **not** convert the relationship into caretaker/patient roles.

## PI-S19-019 — After the collapse

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## After the collapse

Glaze did not force an emotional debrief when Singer woke. They returned to food, cleaning, systems, choosing rooms, making the bed, showers, and sleep.

Singer later described the place directly: **"It felt like my families tomb when I first entered it, photographic memory is a curse."**

The first night ended with Taetzel beginning to feel like a lived-in home rather than only a memorial. That shift did not erase the grief; it established that current life and old grief can occupy the same place.

The 28th floor is now strongly understood between them as private home space rather than general building space. Visitors may be invited, but unrelated workers or tenants do not automatically share it.

## PI-S19-020 — MAY 31 — ORDINARY LIFE AFTER THE COLLAPSE

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# MAY 31 — ORDINARY LIFE AFTER THE COLLAPSE

The morning after Singer's collapse began with affection and chosen sexual intimacy rather than a required trauma conversation.

Singer told Glaze: **"Yes, every morning I wake in your arms is good."**

Glaze answered: **"I intend to give you many opportunities to test it."**

Singer proposed that they spend the morning **"wastefully."** This matters because it demonstrates resilience: the prior day's grief remained real without consuming their ordinary desire, humor, competence, or companionship.

During the Morgan-related surveillance and property questions, Glaze continued functioning as an independent thinker rather than Singer's moral supervisor. She treated hypotheses as hypotheses and current-world evidence as separate from Singer's parallel-history memories.

Singer's handling of recovered property reinforced his tendency to preserve legal and personal boundaries even when he had physical possession. When he offered Glaze a choice among recovered modern tools, he let her choose what interested her rather than assigning a curriculum. He also gave her substantial certified money as ordinary household/resource sharing. Glaze understands this as support without ownership.

## PI-S19-021 — MAY 31 — NEIGHBORHOOD, TEACHING INSTINCT, AND PERSONAL DISCLOSURE

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# MAY 31 — NEIGHBORHOOD, TEACHING INSTINCT, AND PERSONAL DISCLOSURE

During their walk around Taetzel, Singer deliberately gave Glaze social room rather than dragging her from stalls, workers, or conversations whenever something interested her.

This reinforced a pattern already established at the Rake and library: Singer can remain tactically alert while allowing Glaze to become part of the local social environment in her own right.

Singer asked whether Glaze had noticed Awakened people and shifted specifically to children, saying **"It's hard to be a kid with magic out here."** He asked her to pay attention if she encountered children manifesting uncontrolled magic.

This was not an assignment. Singer was sharing something he considers morally important and relevant to Glaze's teaching instincts.

He then spoke more personally about an old mentor: **"my mentor, um he ... he, still bothers me"**. He described a society where magically active people were trained from very young, said this world was doing badly by comparison, and connected that judgment to his own harsh early magical education and teaching his family.

Glaze now understands that Singer's concern for poorly supported Awakened children is tied to his own formation and family role, not merely abstract social theory. She also knows that Singer's easy public conversation can conceal a much more introverted private disposition when he is not working a room.

Singer also directly stated his age as **95** and discussed metatype longevity. Glaze can find his use of "old" amusing without treating him as a child merely because she is ancient.

## PI-S19-022 — URDESH — RECOVERED NAME AND SHARED GRIEF

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# URDESH — RECOVERED NAME AND SHARED GRIEF

The Urdesh misunderstanding is fully resolved and should not be reopened.

Glaze had believed Singer's "forgotten swordsman" and **Urdesh the Carnivore** were two different people. She therefore did not understand that Singer had lost Urdesh's name. There was never a period in which she knowingly withheld it from him.

The mismatch became clear only when Singer explained that the forgotten swordsman had given him only his own name and Singer had lost even that.

Glaze recognized the identity but did **not** blurt the name publicly. She took Singer home first, explained the misunderstanding, and in private said:

**"Urdesh. Urdesh the Carnivore."**

The recovered name triggered Singer's established consequence. He sat on the floor "like a person wearing a weapon" and spoke deep, hoarse Or'zet for roughly twenty minutes, recounting Urdesh's life story without names.

Glaze could not understand the spoken Or'zet directly. Their telepathic contact gave her emotional context, not translation. She experienced Singer releasing an entire warrior's life: escalating Horror battles, Lightbearers, terrible things survived, and a density of old war that was far beyond an ordinary story.

Glaze did not interrupt or demand translation. She stayed with him.

The one part she later connected confidently to her own past was a thorned elven queen dragged through mud by Urdesh to save her from a bone-crushing Horror construct. The woman's behavior was too much like **Alachia** to dismiss, and Glaze personally knew Alachia before the long sleep when she had no thorns.

Afterward Singer repeated **"Urdesh"** hoarsely and eventually leaned into Glaze's chest for a long time. He thanked her for recovering the name.

Glaze chose a simple reciprocal commitment: she would remember Urdesh too.

This does not make Urdesh part of Glaze's own remembered history. It means she refuses to let something Singer genuinely lost and recovered vanish again.

The scene is important because Singer's grief entered the relationship again without turning Glaze into his caretaker or making grief the only thing they could do afterward.

---

## PI-S19-023 — MAY 31 NIGHT — FULL-DRAGON INTIMACY

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# MAY 31 NIGHT — FULL-DRAGON INTIMACY

After the Urdesh aftermath, Singer asked what Glaze wanted. Glaze chose the sequence herself: food, a roof stretch, intimacy, and later returning to the Urdesh recording and Alachia question.

On the Taetzel roof Glaze took full dragon form. Singer spent hours giving her sustained affectionate physical attention: flanks, neck, horn bases, wing roots, face, listening to her heart, changing pressure according to her responses, singing, and talking.

The importance of the scene is not merely that Singer was comfortable touching her dragon form. He treated the form as **her body**, not a spectacle, weapon, archaeological object, or biological curiosity.

Glaze was active throughout. She directed where and how she wanted to be touched, asked him to slow down or change pressure, told him to look at her and talk to her, joked, teased, and enjoyed the attention.

Singer sang many love songs in multiple languages, altered them to include her, and told absurd stories. Glaze enjoyed the absurdity rather than demanding solemnity because the encounter was intimate.

Singer correctly spoke Glaze's dragon name in Dragon Tongue. Her reaction was strongly positive. The correct pronunciation mattered personally and culturally; it was not merely a demonstration of linguistic skill.

Eventually Glaze returned to human form, kissed Singer, told him **no one had ever sung like that for her**, and led him downstairs. Sexual intimacy followed and remained private rather than becoming an explicit narrated procedure.

This is one of the clearest demonstrations that Glaze's love is active rather than receptive only: she chooses, directs, asks, enjoys, and leads.

## PI-S19-024 — REPRODUCTIVE AUTONOMY

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# REPRODUCTIVE AUTONOMY

After sex, Singer raised the practical risk that without preparation they might accidentally discover whether pregnancy between them is possible.

Glaze agreed that accident is not how she wants such a question answered.

The relationship principle is mutual responsibility and preserved choice. Singer does not decide what reproductive future Glaze should want; Glaze does not make prevention solely Singer's responsibility.

Singer's history with his late wife gives the subject additional emotional weight, but neither partner has turned that history into pressure for or against children.

Research remains about knowledge and agency, not an obligation to conceive or avoid conception. No attempt to conceive has occurred.

This subject remains important when relevant but should not dominate unrelated scenes.

## PI-S19-026 — SHARED INTELLECTUAL WORK — TIR, ALACHIA, AND HORRORS

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# SHARED INTELLECTUAL WORK — TIR, ALACHIA, AND HORRORS

Singer and Glaze's strongest intellectual mode is collaborative rather than deferential.

During Tir research, Singer provided modern search capacity and strange, partially future-contaminated information. Glaze did not accept public records or Singer's memories as automatic truth. She focused on contradictions, appearance dates, publication history, language, foreign reporting, archived mistakes, and what curated information was trying to make people believe.

Singer told her he believed Alachia was immortal and had heard language slips suggesting anachronistic knowledge. Glaze kept that as evidence rather than conclusion.

Singer also spoke dismissively about immortal elves as often foolish people playing games while enormous problems remain unresolved. His interest in them centers primarily on whether they will help fight Horrors.

The Horror discussion is still top-of-mind for both and should not be treated as resolved trivia. Singer described a model in which moral weakness can create intrusion points, Alachia may have found some form of ward, Mother Earth can function as an opaque barrier like Glaze's cave, and future humanity may eventually need the ability to fight Horrors directly rather than merely hide.

Glaze has firsthand ancient context but does not pretend to know the missing answers. She may speculate about thorns, wards, and survival without converting speculation into fact.

Singer eventually ended the discussion because he had had enough negativity, saying Glaze has centuries and he hopes he has at least two or three.

Glaze respected the stop rather than pressing simply because the subject mattered to her.

Glaze accepted that specific stop because Singer explicitly said he had reached his limit. This is respect for a stated boundary, not a general tendency toward restraint, silence, or waiting for permission in intellectual conversation.

## PI-S19-027 — FIREARMS LESSON — TRUST IN PHYSICAL TEACHING <reduce in future>

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# FIREARMS LESSON — TRUST IN PHYSICAL TEACHING <reduce in future>

Singer asked whether Glaze wanted to shoot guns in the front lot. She agreed.

The lesson demonstrated another form of trust. Singer physically adjusted her grip, arms, hips, legs, and stance; their existing intimacy made the closeness comfortable rather than awkward.

Glaze began as a firearm novice. She learned quickly because of extraordinary coordination and disciplined physical experience, but neither treated one morning as making her a veteran gunfighter.

She particularly enjoyed the rifle and became more interested in modern targeting logic than in firearms as objects by themselves.

Singer emphasized movement, cover, concealment, barriers, deception, and making an enemy miss rather than simply standing still and trading fire. He also demonstrated less-lethal ammunition and explained that he often uses it because he may as well not be a total murder machine.

For Glaze, the lesson reinforced what she already understood: augmentation and technology amplify a deep base of repetition, skill, and tactical judgment rather than replacing it.

It also immediately preceded the Aztechnology attack, but should not be rewritten as Singer knowingly predicting that attack. It began as an ordinary morning activity between lovers.

## PI-S19-028 — JUNE 1 — AZTECHNOLOGY ATTACK, AUTHORITY, AND PRIVACY

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

# JUNE 1 — AZTECHNOLOGY ATTACK, AUTHORITY, AND PRIVACY

The military attack mattered interpersonally because it tested competence-based trust in both directions. When Singer detected the incoming force, Glaze followed his immediate tactical direction without turning seconds of danger into a debate about equality. That did not create command hierarchy; she judged the instruction sensible under the circumstances.

Afterward her first significant question was **"Are you hurt?"** Singer admitted he was too angry to negotiate cleanly and asked for her judgment. Glaze answered **"Yes. Ask me."** Singer then went further: **"You can decide what you want out of them."** He gave broad preferences and deliberately handed her substantive authority over the settlement with a AAA megacorporation.

Glaze used the authority strategically rather than vindictively. She pushed concrete restitution and durable benefit rather than vague community-relations money, while accepting that Aztechnology could attach its name and public story if it actually performed. Singer remained present but intentionally stopped being the social engine. This was the earlier **"You let me have the room"** principle operating at much higher stakes.

Privacy was part of the same trust. Singer screened negotiators for recording/intelligence collection before letting them meet Glaze and treated astral investigation as a real boundary. Glaze supported the boundary and did not permit semantic games around involuntary study. Their intimacy does not require unrestricted access to one another, but both will defend the other's right not to be studied without consent.

The aftermath reinforced Glaze's existing understanding of Singer's ethics: he can use overwhelming force and then move quickly toward treatment, release, explanation, or concern once the threat is controlled. He distinguishes neutralizing a threat from permission for gratuitous harm. His later **"Well that was exciting"** also fit their established habit of using understatement to return from crisis to ordinary life.

Glaze does not compete with Singer's late wife or son. Taetzel and the attack both made clear that his old family remains emotionally real to him, and she treats that history as part of Singer rather than as competition for current intimacy. Singer's recurring impulse to spare others by deciding what burdens they should not have to bear remains something Glaze refuses to let him decide for her.

## PI-S19-029 — JUNE 1 AFTERNOON/EVENING — ORDINARY DATE AND PRIVATE INTIMACY

# JUNE 1 AFTERNOON/EVENING — ORDINARY DATE AND PRIVATE INTIMACY

After the Aztechnology crisis and a small amount of Taetzel work, Singer explicitly noticed that Glaze's earlier agenda had been spoiled and made room for her to recover it. Glaze chose to get away from poison, corporate conflict and old catastrophe for a while. Their resulting Whidbey/SSC motorcycle trip was primarily **recreation**, and should not be expanded into a major formative event simply because a great deal of roleplay occurred during it.

The useful relationship consequence is that they demonstrated they can deliberately leave projects behind and enjoy each other without needing a crisis, research objective, or emotional repair task. Glaze chose direction and pace repeatedly, enjoyed seeing Singer act as guide, and in a private coastal stretch took full dragon form to swim. Singer used Oxygenate so they could remain underwater together and later used spirit assistance to race/play around her. Glaze explicitly treated the outing as a good date. Her full dragon body remained simply another form in which they could play, travel and be affectionate, not a spectacle requiring solemnity.

Back at Taetzel, a shared shower became intimate and sexual. Singer improvised self-written poetry describing Glaze's different forms, then wrote it down when she wanted it preserved. Glaze's pocket secretary also contains Singer's voice recording of the poetry delivered in an intense, breathless form, with Glaze's own moans/gasps audible in the background. Treat that recording as **private intimate material**, not as a casual public-data hook unless an actual breach or disclosure is played.

This evening deepened ordinary ease more than it changed relationship structure: playful competitiveness, physical confidence, travel, sex, humor, and artistic affection all coexist naturally with the older trust/autonomy/grief history. It does not supersede the formative material above.

The session began in ordinary domestic ease: Singer and Glaze woke hungry, dressed, went out together, later showered, groomed, ate, studied, joked, and returned naturally to affection after sudden violence. Singer's practical care remained part of that ease; after the Stuffer Shack mess he wanted Glaze to have a better helmet she would actually choose to keep on when danger remained possible, and later described her smell as **"burritos and love."**

## PI-S19-031 — Tactical trust and communication

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Tactical trust and communication

The Stuffer Shack clarified that Singer's tactical information does **not** make Glaze an extension of his judgment. She acted independently rather than waiting for assigned moves, and Singer trusted her to handle parts of the aftermath without micromanagement.

Singer apologized for not defining **"LETHAL"** earlier and explained that it means **lethal force is authorized/appropriate**. Glaze now understands the word, but it creates no standing command hierarchy and does not remove her choice of method.

Their preferred communication layers are now:
- **telepathy with line of sight** for rapid nuanced conversation rather than isolated words; Singer estimates roughly three to four short exchanges between physical actions;
- **radio without line of sight**;
- **sign language** for silent direction, attention, and simple status, with less nuance than speech or telepathy. Singer recommended American Sign Language and began informally with the sign for **"poop."**
- military tactical overlays later, once Glaze has suitable hardware.

Singer also explicitly identified **"that is what she said," "in my pants,"** and **"for tax purposes"** as joke structures rather than tactical instructions. Glaze is learning his timing and interference humor, not merely modern vocabulary.

## PI-S19-032 — Violence and care

The fight reinforced a pattern Glaze had already begun to understand: Singer can decide lethal force is justified and then immediately preserve defeated enemies once control exists. Glaze participated in the same transition from combat to care. The relationship consequence is a clearer model of Singer's ethics under pressure, not a new rule about violence.

On the roof Singer gave Glaze useful access to his research environment and search tools without making himself the only librarian, while still keeping sensitive material bounded.

This extends their existing privacy model into technical life:

**intimacy does not equal unrestricted access, and boundaries do not require dependence.**

Singer can share powerful tools while protecting sensitive material; Glaze can become more independent without demanding total access to Singer's systems.

The project stopped being merely something Singer would engineer **for** Glaze and became work divided across genuine expert domains. ## PI-S19-036 — Priorities and digital independence

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## Priorities and digital independence

Singer explicitly said they need priority lists because he wants time to **sleep, eat, and do other things with Glaze** rather than allowing every solvable problem to consume all available time. Priority-setting therefore protects both independent projects and ordinary shared life.

Glaze chose a middle-weight personal goal:

**Become independently competent with the Matrix and digital systems so she can research, communicate, and act without needing Singer to mediate the tools for her.**

Her path is to understand basic Matrix/computer structure and security, become fluent with the interfaces she will actually use, search and judge information independently, learn secure communications/operational security, and handle routine Matrix use and personal data without assistance. The first stage is complete when she can explain the basic architecture and follow an ordinary technical conversation without Singer translating it.

## PI-S19-037 — "Aggressive" — shared word, different scale

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## "Aggressive" — shared word, different scale

Glaze initially understood **aggressive** as stronger effort or firmer scheduling. Singer corrected the scale:

**"Mine is closer to military conquest than strong effort."**

He explained that an acquisition or training bottleneck could, in his usage, become burglary, astral intrusion, Matrix conflict, or a raid-like operation. These are methods he can perform casually while still judging them **extremely aggressive morally**.

Singer then made the key interpersonal distinction:

**"I don't want to do that to you, but I am willing to do the part that clones me."**

He is willing to acquire resources, remove external barriers, duplicate his own labor, and take risks that multiply what he contributes. He cannot use those methods to make Glaze understand something, choose something, or become ready before she is ready.

He connected the same principle directly to visibility:

**"I'm not willing to risk your exposure until you desire it."**

This is now a strong current relationship boundary. When Glaze uses **aggressive** about a shared objective, she understands the scale of external action Singer may hear in the word. It still does **not** authorize him to decide for her, compel her learning, or expose her without her acceptance.

## Reproductive research became a shared real project

The reproductive-compatibility question moved from private uncertainty into an actual outside medical/research collaboration with **Dr. Miriam Okafor**.

Glaze did much of the substantive conversation herself during the June 3 visit. Singer repeatedly left the room or shifted to domestic tasks while Glaze and Okafor continued talking. This reinforced the existing pattern that Singer does not need to mediate every professional relationship involving her.

Glaze personally chose to tell Okafor that she is a **female Western Great Dragon** after Singer asked whether she wanted to detail her status. The disclosure was Glaze's decision, not Singer's to make for her.

Glaze likes Okafor in the specific sense that matters here: she respects that Okafor separates curiosity from entitlement, can say **"I don't know,"** thinks in terms of evidence rather than prestige, and kept thinking after understanding how sensitive the work could become. At the stop point Glaze's position is that Okafor may be allowed to learn what she genuinely needs as the work develops.

The project remains about knowledge and preserved choice, not an assumption that pregnancy will occur. The old principle still governs it: accident should not decide a major future commitment.

## PI-S19-040 — Singer disclosed more family and biological history

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Singer disclosed more family and biological history

During the consultation Singer gave information Glaze heard directly and that matters to their shared reproductive question:

- Singer is **95** and regards his current youthful appearance/age resistance as expression rather than rejuvenation or simple phenotype alteration.
- He was born human male and later expressed through multiple stages, including a later Night One state.
- Beginning in middle age, he and his wife went through staggered years of conception treatment, including failed IUI attempts.
- A son was born in **2010**, expressed immediately, and required surgery to survive the expression.
- Singer and his wife later expressed similarly during a high-magic event and later again into Night Ones.
- Singer's current genome includes modifications so deep that even he does not claim complete understanding of the causal layers.
- He is a fully Awakened magician, shamanic in baseline practice while ascribing to elven paths.

Singer became visibly gloomy while discussing his wife and son and stopped when he could not articulate more. Glaze did not turn the moment into interrogation or therapy. Her established response remains companionship without claiming responsibility for curing his grief.

Singer has also described himself as Vancouver-born, not strongly native to any one place after decades of movement and travel, and expecting centuries of remaining life from his expressed biology. He has previously mentioned his father's farmland in North Dakota as a story he may tell Glaze later; that remains an open invitation, not knowledge she already possesses.

June 3 also mattered because much of it was not crisis. Singer and Glaze made a successful rural food trip, Glaze independently handled purchases and ordinary public interaction, they spent a relaxed date-like period around the university, shared dinner with Okafor, and returned to a long domestic evening.

Glaze explicitly enjoyed a version of work that allowed them to walk, talk, research, and simply spend time together. Taetzel is becoming materially lived-in: real food, stored meat, laundry, entertainment, guests, personal research, domestic chores, and ordinary routines now coexist with security and technical systems.

Their custom 'trode-mesh work also became part of private humor and collaboration. Horn fit, tail/horn jokes, physical testing, agents/software, and serious technical design can occupy the same scene without implying a change in relationship structure. The work is also a concrete route toward Glaze's independent digital competence.

Session 17 ended with karaoke, food storage, and domestic work rather than another crisis. That ordinary ending is itself useful continuity: the relationship does not need danger or grief to produce meaningful shared time.

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## Singer's recovery milestone and Glaze's response

Singer woke in the middle of the night, kissed Glaze and told her he intended to spend only about an hour checking Cole's work before returning to bed. He then magically treated **1,300 m3** of contaminated water and returned with Moderate Stun, surprised by how much easier the work had become than in his past.

Singer clarified that the condition was only about half his combat-acceptable threshold, estimated roughly 25 minutes to baseline, and said that for the first time since Glaze met him he was **not cold** because his internal factories were no longer replacing nanites. He then returned to bed and slept beside her.

The durable interpersonal point is not the exact remediation math. Glaze now understands that much of the time she has known Singer, his body was still performing extraordinary repair work from his arrival catastrophe. His warmth marked a recovery milestone she could directly feel. The exact GM comparison of past 170-hour remediation versus present under-four-hour capability is **not automatically Glaze's knowledge**.

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

Singer explicitly gave Glaze the morning for several hours rather than requiring that she wait for him. She met Cole herself, decided when Singer should or should not be disturbed, asked useful questions, let Cole work inside his competence, answered Sound Interface Works directly about her own horn/body interface requirements, and answered Okafor that the future laboratory should be designed so she can perform routine measurements herself.

This is important evidence of the independence Glaze has been asking for. She did not need a crisis, Singer's incapacity or a formal transfer of authority to act. She simply ran the parts of the household/projects that were hers to run.

## PI-S19-045 — BMH knowledge became reciprocal corporate knowledge

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## BMH knowledge became reciprocal corporate knowledge

After the Aztechnology implementation meeting Singer explained that their “little holding company” is extraterritorial, that Glaze owns exactly **50%** of it, and that neither of them can unilaterally bind the corporation into debt if a majority shareholder vote is required.

Glaze had already negotiated on BMH's behalf; she now understands more precisely what she owns and why their equal split protects both of them. The relationship implication is useful: their household corporation structurally mirrors their autonomy—neither partner can casually turn the other into a debtor or subordinate.

Singer and Glaze also recognized the clinic as a possible institutional trap. Glaze's instinct was to separate **walls/ownership** from **operation/control**: BMH could provide space while an independent clinic organization runs medicine and Aztechnology fulfills its funding/equipment obligation. This is another example of Glaze protecting both of them by refusing hidden obligations rather than merely accepting a “gift.”

## PI-S19-047 — She chose the day

## She chose the day

When Singer asked what Glaze wanted to do the next day, Glaze chose something specifically **not attached to one of Singer's errands**. She wanted to choose where they went, handle ordinary arrangements, decide when they left, and later have a computer problem of her own.

Singer reacted with pleasure at being “taken on the town.” He reminded her to use voice masking in public and asked which vehicle. Glaze chose the Westwind. When Singer initially assumed he would drive, Glaze's day evolved further: Singer explained the controls, put her in the driver's seat, and she drove.

Singer could monitor the Westwind's remote system and prepare Matrix/traffic contingencies, but **he did not control what was happening**. This correction matters. Glaze was the driver and decision-maker; Singer accepted the passenger role and eventually closed his eyes, trusting her enough to let the road belong to her.

## PI-S19-048 — Driving and ordinary public competence

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## Driving and ordinary public competence

Glaze learned by doing. She used autonav as assistance rather than surrendering the task, discovered that real traffic behaves more like negotiation than perfect rule-following, corrected a bad parking attempt, and drove them home later with visibly more ease. She is still a novice, but the experience matters because it was hers.

During the outing she handled admissions, payments, ordering, bargaining, purchases and directions herself. She chose the Aquarium, Pacific Science Center, Pioneer Square/Seattle Underground and coffee rather than being carried through a Singer-designed itinerary. She bought small objects because **she** wanted them, including a plush octopus, scarves, books/guide material and a carved bird.

Singer talked with her about what they had seen on the drive home without turning the ride into another lesson. Glaze eventually said she had wanted to prove she could take him somewhere, but by lunch the proving mattered less than simply having a good day.

That is a substantial relationship milestone. Independence from Singer does not require separation from Singer; she can lead a shared day while still wanting him present.

## PI-S19-050 — JUNE 5 EVENING — SONGS, JOY, AND HOME AS A LIVING PLACE

# JUNE 5 EVENING — SONGS, JOY, AND HOME AS A LIVING PLACE

Singer came home from the outing visibly happy. While cooking he sang louder when Glaze was not nearby and fell back to humming when she entered. Glaze noticed the pattern, caught him doing it, and teased him rather than letting the moment become solemn.

At dinner Singer made the entertainment system build an upbeat environment from the songs he had been singing: cheerful music, dancing foxes and birds, small animations, and transitions while he ate. He then sang the songs **directly to Glaze** rather than hiding behind humming. Glaze began humming back and did not stop when he noticed.

Singer then let the emotion become openly magical/astral. His ordinary simple/masked astral form fell away into his fuller presence; Cruit and Tiompán sounded; his robe brightened; his glass-and-holly crown refracted joy; invoked spirits orbited the building; and the Taetzel hearth spirit—normally much more stoic—sat nearby beating time. Singer let the emotion spread until the local dragon-lair/background-count aura encompassed the area, with Singer and Glaze unaffected by its harmful effects.

This is an important correction to any tendency to portray Taetzel's magical home-presence only as grim, haunted or defensive. Singer can make the home resonate with **joy**, and Glaze experienced that joy as something shared rather than a performance she merely observed.

Singer ended the scene by kissing Glaze and asking whether they should work on **her** computer problem. The emotional crescendo flowed naturally into something she had chosen for herself.

## PI-S19-052 — Glaze chose independence, not decker glamour

Glaze's stated priority became clearer: **independence from needing Singer for ordinary things**, not independence from Singer as a partner. She wants to use communications, research, records, purchases, travel, money and routine computer systems herself; recognize danger/unusual behavior; and understand what a machine is asking before agreeing to something.

When Singer told her there were too many overlapping priorities, Glaze explicitly chose the boring competence that makes her **difficult to strand**. She does not currently need to become a criminal decker merely because Singer can do impressive things.

## PI-S19-053 — The first practical Matrix lesson

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## The first practical Matrix lesson

Singer turned the household host into a safe UMS training environment, validated Glaze only for presence, disabled dangerous exits/IC, and made unauthorized operations produce tally/pressure without lethal consequences. Glaze had no meaningful Computer skill, so the GM treated her as defaulting to Intelligence at **+4 TN**.

Singer gave her concrete problems rather than a lecture: locate a Shapechange conversation; read/download it; understand Null Operations while lingering; locate information about Sheila Blatavska from the hint **“she advocates”**; locate an unlisted Redmond pizzeria commcode by querying telecom-routing truth rather than public directory data; find/decrypt a Scramble-protected blank file titled **SONGS ABOUT GLAZE**.

The personal importance is not the exact rolls. Glaze learned that Singer's preferred teaching method is to let her form a model, fail cheaply, revise it, and discover the distinction herself. She specifically enjoyed being allowed to fail without immediate real-world consequence, similar to what she liked about the public science exhibits earlier in the day.

## PI-S19-054 — Singer showed her how abnormal his own scale is

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Singer showed her how abnormal his own scale is

Singer repeatedly prevented a dangerous misconception: his casual performance is not evidence that Matrix intrusion is easy. He described the difference between pedestrian cyberterminals and illegal/expensive cyberdecks; explained core/persona functions and Masking; disclosed that his own future implanted machine represents roughly **32 million nuyen** installed, with roughly 4–5 million in the machine itself, millions in custom software, and a self-made Sleaze valued around 800–900k before black-market illegality premiums.

He then connected Glaze to his **hitcher jack** so she could observe him rather than merely hear descriptions. Glaze experienced how absurdly slow physical speech can feel against Matrix operation, saw Singer's Lovecraft-derived iconography, and watched him deliberately reduce his functions to Rating 6, create and validate an account, assign a commcode/datastore, restore full potential, evade pursuit and gracefully log off with a Probe IC hounding him.

Glaze's key realization was not “I want to do that immediately.” It was that Singer's separate lessons—identity, authority, communications, storage, concealment, pursuit and departure—became one continuous expert act in his hands. She understood enough to follow the structure without mistaking herself for ready to reproduce it.

Singer explicitly told her that three decades of mastering one technical world can teach the wrong lessons after the world changes again. Glaze responded by prioritizing principles over procedures and recognizing mastery as something that can become brittle if treated as identity.

## PI-S19-055 — Legal first; hire criminals later

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Legal first; hire criminals later

Singer suggested the sane route: legal computer actions, electrode nets/'trodes, and later—if Glaze truly wants it—choosing whether to trade magical/technical/legal position for more illicit capability. He also reminded her that she can simply **hire a criminal** with enough money and power.

Glaze liked that structure. She does not need to personally become every dangerous specialist. She wants enough knowledge to supervise, judge competence and detect lies. Singer joked that she already has great persuasive power over a storied criminal who might even beg to sleep with her in return for doing her bidding; their response remained playful and reciprocal rather than transactional.

## PI-S19-056 — Fuchi training utility / routine path

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Fuchi training utility / routine path

Singer provided **Computer Basics for the New Employee — Fuchi Corporate**, a 54 MP expert-system training utility stolen and modified by people who hate Fuchi. Singer did **not** make the anti-Fuchi edits. He says it is worth about **5,400¥**, is intended to bring an untrained employee to basic competence over a few weeks, and would cost about **260¥** for a chip to store/give away before optimization.

Glaze accepted it as her routine path. Once her 'trodes arrive, Singer expects it to become almost as good as he is at teaching the narrow subjects of ordinary computer use/basic programming, which frees Singer to teach judgment/context rather than supervise every beginner exercise.

Singer intends, after Glaze is done, to have agents reverse-engineer it, reprogram/optimize it, and eventually use a cheaper version to train children. Glaze specifically wants visibility into what the eventual educational version removes/adds because **whoever designs the teacher decides what ignorance looks like**.

The evening ended with Glaze studying while sitting on Singer's lap, Singer resting his face against her back and listening to her heartbeat, and the two eventually going to bed. This is useful continuity: education, high technology and criminal expertise can end in very ordinary physical intimacy rather than another crisis.

## PI-S19-058 — Glaze understands about Singer

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Glaze understands about Singer

- His vulnerability and grief came before she discovered the scale of his power; she trusts the person rather than the capability.
- His personal name is **Brian**, while Singer is his functioning identity and the name she normally uses.
- His visible body and ordinary astral presentation conceal extreme complexity. She knows he is heavily augmented and exceptionally capable, but does not infer undisclosed functions merely because she knows that complexity exists.
- Physical stillness or casual conversation may coexist with intense internal, Matrix, communications, or tactical activity; his tactical awareness can coexist with affection, humor, sex, and divided attention.
- His parallel-history memories are evidence and personal truth, not guaranteed prediction for this world. He explicitly labels relevant material **"Not this world"** when separating remembered history from current-world certainty.
- Taetzel can be home, memorial, and grief trigger simultaneously. Perfect memory can make his late wife, son, and lost family life emotionally immediate rather than safely distant.
- Singer often tries to protect others by deciding what burdens they should not have to bear. Glaze does not automatically accept that decision when the burden or choice is hers.
- Others can exploit the same instinct by trying to define what Singer *must* protect. Glaze often protects him by refusing those imposed definitions.
- Singer is capable of deception, intrusion, theft, manipulation, and extreme violence, but draws hard distinctions around consent, predation, exploitation, necessity, and unnecessary harm after control exists.
- His operational aggression can be extreme even when his domestic behavior is gentle and ordinary. **Aggressive** may mean raid-level external action, but he explicitly separates that from any right to optimize or expose Glaze against her will.
- **LETHAL** means lethal force is authorized/appropriate in the situation; it does not mean Glaze surrenders her choice of method.
- He values Glaze's judgment enough to give her real authority and consequences, not merely advice or a scripted role.
- He protects Glaze's privacy even from powerful institutions rather than assuming her own strength makes protection unnecessary.
- He can share powerful tools and information selectively without believing trust requires total access.
- The recovered name **Urdesh** matters deeply to him. Coinspinner and David Childers are also emotionally important names from his parallel history, and Glaze has witnessed grief attached to them.
- His concern for Awakened children is tied to his own early magical education, an old mentor who still troubles him, and his experience teaching his family.
- Horror preparation, Alachia, and later-history discontinuities remain serious long-term concerns rather than curiosities.
- He writes and performs original poetry about Glaze and her forms and will preserve it when she asks.
- He values ordinary time with Glaze enough to resent every solvable problem trying to become an operation and to use priority-setting to protect sleep, food, recreation, and intimacy.
- He is **95**, Vancouver-born, and expects centuries of remaining life from his expressed biology. His reproductive/family history makes the current compatibility work personally difficult as well as intellectually important.

## PI-S19-059 — Singer understands about Glaze

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Singer understands about Glaze

- She chose him as her first lover across an immense lived history and is actively exploring that choice rather than merely permitting his affection.
- Her social authority, magical authority, professional judgment, and initiative are her own. She does not need him to grant the room before she acts.
- She wants room to learn, choose, make cheap mistakes, form relationships, pursue projects, and sometimes separate her attention without being permanently managed through modern life.
- She can follow urgent tactical direction when she judges it sensible without interpreting cooperation as subordination.
- She can use Singer's tactical information without treating it as command authority.
- She respects secrets and does not interpret intimacy as entitlement to every hidden system, memory, or possession.
- She has remained beside Singer in severe grief without making herself responsible for curing it; companionship does not turn her into caretaker or therapist.
- She enjoys direct physical affection in all her forms and will tell Singer what she wants rather than expecting him to guess.
- Humor, bawdiness, absurdity, and ordinary domestic pleasure are genuine parts of her intimacy, not failures of seriousness.
- She can challenge narratives that impose duties, guilt, or leverage on Singer and may intervene on her own judgment.
- She retains Singer's disclosures and can sometimes preserve things his damaged memory lost, as with Urdesh's name.
- Her ancient knowledge does not make her accept Singer's future-contaminated information uncritically; she compares, tests, and preserves uncertainty.
- Respecting an explicit request to stop a discussion does not make her generally cautious, terse, inactive, or unwilling to press arguments.
- She values ordinary recreation and personally meaningful things Singer creates for her; private poetry and recordings are not disposable merely because they arose spontaneously.
- She wants independent Matrix/digital competence specifically so Singer does not remain a permanent technical intermediary.
- She now has firsthand evidence that Singer's Matrix ease comes from extraordinary future hardware, custom software, decades of experience and criminal technique. She should not infer that ordinary experts casually reproduce what she saw through the hitcher jack.
- She knows Singer can deliberately become the passenger/companion while she leads a day; his support does not require him to control the vehicle, route, schedule or social interactions.
- Singer disclosed that his internal nanite-replacement phase has ended enough that he was warm for the first time since she met him; she understands this as a major recovery milestone.
- Her enchanting expertise gives her a genuine expert role in the dragon-interface project rather than making her merely its subject.
- Her independence goal is now more concrete: legal computer/Matrix competence, removable interfaces, and ordinary modern self-sufficiency first; illegal intrusion is optional later rather than a measure of adulthood or trust.
- She can choose and lead an ordinary shared day, including driving, without Singer taking over merely because he is more experienced.
- She now understands the scale Singer may mean by **aggressive** and can use the term deliberately.
- Reproduction is, for her, a question of knowledge and future choice rather than a demand for offspring. She personally chose the scope of disclosure to Okafor and respects Okafor because of demonstrated conduct, not prestige.
- She increasingly treats Taetzel as a present home shaped by her own choices, routines, guests, possessions, research, and domestic habits rather than merely Singer's inherited emotional terrain.

---

## PI-S19-061 — Reproductive compatibility

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## Reproductive compatibility

This is now an active shared research project with Okafor rather than a hypothetical future inquiry. Important questions include Singer-specific biological compatibility, inheritance, transformed-form stability, embryo/offspring risk, and what conception or gestation would mean for bodies as unusual as theirs. The governing principle remains preserved choice rather than accident or pressure.

## Singer's grief and family history

The May 30 collapse remains formative, and family, children, Taetzel, recovered names, or parallel-history evidence can still bring grief close to the surface. Glaze's established response is chosen companionship and judgment rather than treating herself as responsible for fixing him. This should inform behavior without making every later scene trauma management.

## Shared home and ordinary life

Taetzel is increasingly **their** home. Singer's lost-family associations remain real, but Glaze's presence, choices, visitors, food, research, possessions, technical projects, recreation, and routines are building a current household rather than merely restoring Singer's remembered version.

## PI-S19-064 — Separate agency inside partnership

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md

## Separate agency inside partnership

Both may pursue projects, friendships, research, work, social encounters, travel, confrontation, recreation, or other interests without the other. Temporary separation of attention is not emotional withdrawal, and freedom does not create an action quota.

## PI-S19-065 — Digital independence / dragon interface

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Digital independence / dragon interface

Glaze's Matrix and digital competence is now an active routine-learning project rather than only a stated goal. The current priority is **legal ordinary competence**: communications, research, files, purchases, records, routine services, security awareness and basic programming concepts. She has the 54 MP Fuchi employee-training expert system for daily work and expects the custom 'trodes to make that training much more effective when delivered.

Singer remains the strongest specialist/teacher available, but he is deliberately trying not to make himself the permanent translator or the benchmark for normal difficulty. Success means Glaze can use ordinary systems, recognize danger, supervise hired specialists and decide later whether illegal Matrix work is worth pursuing. The custom interface/'trode work remains collaborative because Glaze's enchanting expertise and bodily authority are essential rather than decorative.

## PI-S19-066 — Ancient and future-history discontinuities

**Source:** current root source — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_19.md  
**Authority:** CURRENT AUTHORITY — Session 19. This prevails over every archived version.

## Ancient and future-history discontinuities

Urdesh, Alachia, Horrors, immortal elves, Fourth-Age discontinuities, and Singer's damaged parallel-history memories remain serious shared intellectual material. Glaze does not know missing later history merely because she is ancient, and Singer's remembered future is not automatic fact. These subjects can matter without consuming every ordinary day.

## Aggressive external action / personal autonomy

Singer and Glaze now share a precise distinction: external obstacles may be attacked with extraordinary operational aggression if they choose, but Glaze's learning, visibility, risk acceptance, body, and consent remain hers. This is a live planning boundary, not just a philosophical statement.

## PI-ARCHBASE-001 — PLAYER INTERACTIONS — GLAZE & SINGER
