## PI-ARCH5-013 — SINGER AS GLAZE KNOWS HIM

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# SINGER AS GLAZE KNOWS HIM

Over the cave period Singer told or demonstrated that:
- he was born human;
- later expressed as elf;
- later Night One;
- underwent additional magically driven genetic/morphic changes;
- his present body includes extensive cyberware, bioware, geneware, nanoware, and other experimental systems;
- much of the work was performed in black-tech environments;
- he retains very strong magic despite extensive alteration.

Glaze learned that Singer's body is a poor guide to his capabilities.

She has observed or been told about:
- unusual strength and speed;
- very high pain tolerance;
- broad sensory capability;
- unusual scent and taste sensitivity;
- hyperspectral vision;
- advanced voice duplication/masking;
- fingerprint and identity-spoofing capability;
- internal computing;
- Matrix access;
- tactical systems;
- communications;
- simsense;
- rigging;
- surveillance and counter-surveillance systems.

Singer's normal astral presentation is heavily Masked and intentionally ordinary.

Glaze has also seen more of his true/unmasked astral presentation and magical imagery than most people are allowed to see.

She respects his choice to conceal it.

---

## PI-ARCH5-014 — TEACHING / MODERN INTERPRETATION

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# TEACHING / MODERN INTERPRETATION

Singer became Glaze's main modern teacher.

He has taught or explained:
- electricity;
- batteries;
- electronics;
- phones/commlinks;
- software;
- pilot programs;
- smart frames;
- Matrix concepts;
- digital information;
- electronic money;
- identity;
- verification;
- simsense;
- modern universities;
- modern magical scholarship;
- global logistics;
- population scale;
- propaganda;
- advertising;
- social media;
- modern security;
- modern policing;
- modern corporations;
- transport;
- lodging;
- public behavior;
- clothing and presentation;
- restaurants and service conventions;
- modern racial/metatype prejudice;
- SIN/SINless exclusion;
- organlegging and related predatory economies.

Singer often bridges unfamiliar concepts through knowledge Glaze already has.

Glaze increasingly asks specific questions rather than broad basic ones.

Examples of the level of questions she has reached include:
- how housekeeping authority differs from security authority;
- what a lock actually controls when authorized staff can bypass it;
- how infrastructure regulates heat and water;
- how media framing changes interpretation without direct falsehood;
- how magical security creates choke points;
- what programs actually understand.

Singer explicitly encouraged Glaze to question him more.

Glaze has taken that seriously.

## PI-ARCH5-015 — PROGRAMS / MACHINES / SINGER'S ROLE

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# PROGRAMS / MACHINES / SINGER'S ROLE

During lodge Matrix/archive work, Glaze saw Singer reorganize large bodies of information quickly and use multiple agents/programs.

She initially attributed too much agency to the software.

Singer corrected this.

The principle Glaze retained is:

**Programs aid. Machines enable. Skill and adaptability are human.**

Singer's agents and frames are powerful tools, not automatically independent scholars or sapient minds.

Glaze also learned that Singer's physical stillness can conceal rapid internal or Matrix activity.

She does not automatically interpret silence, closed eyes, or a delayed physical response as confusion or disengagement.

---

## PI-ARCH5-016 — MUSIC

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# MUSIC

Glaze has seen music function for Singer as:
- entertainment;
- humor;
- social connection;
- teaching;
- emotional expression;
- magical/ritual practice;
- simsense material;
- self-regulation.

She does not treat every song as a confession.

The mountain performance of **"Walking on Sunshine"** was different.

Singer performed it unmasked and tearful, and the emotional content was visible beneath the cheerful song.

Later lodge music was often playful:
- karaoke;
- altered lyrics;
- situational songs;
- absurd jokes.

Glaze has seen that Singer can be genuinely joyful while still carrying profound grief.

After intense cheerful performance, he can still remain visibly sad underneath it.

Glaze does not automatically demand discussion when that happens.

# HUMOR

Singer's humor includes:
- dry understatement;
- self-deprecation;
- technical absurdity;
- mock-liturgical Latin;
- plumbing jokes;
- improvised songs;
- absurd lyric changes;
- crude signing;
- social mockery used to invite inclusion;
- the happy tapeworm/passenger joke.

Glaze's own humor with Singer has generally been:
- dry;
- regal;
- lightly teasing;
- occasionally taking his absurd premise seriously for one beat.

"Plumbing adequacy" became private humor.

Singer explicitly asked not to be made ashamed of vulnerable delirious behavior.

Teasing around it therefore remains affectionate rather than humiliating.

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md

# MONEY / INDEPENDENCE

Singer gave Glaze a certified credstick containing **8,864¥**.

He explained the approximate practical value.

When Glaze noticed the gift represented a large fraction of his anonymous liquid resources, Singer said:

> "I won't say I won't miss having it, but I'd miss you not having it more."

Glaze accepted the money.

She intends to use modern resources carefully while she learns their scale.

The gift is not a debt, bond, ownership marker, or patronage claim.

# PUBLIC SOCIAL PARTNERSHIP

On the mountain, at the lodge, and later in Seattle, Glaze watched Singer interact with ordinary people.

She has seen him:
- approach strangers openly;
- sing while approaching;
- use humor to normalize oddity;
- provide practical help;
- gather information conversationally;
- switch among languages;
- joke with orks and trolls;
- turn transactions into social exchanges;
- redirect interaction toward Glaze rather than permanently speaking for her.

Glaze learned that Singer's unusual appearance produces a broad range of responses:
- curiosity;
- fascination;
- friendliness;
- discomfort;
- attraction;
- prejudice;
- hostility.

Singer's social skill can alter reactions without making prejudice disappear.

At the lodge, Singer joked with:
- **Marek**, an ork construction foreman and climber;
- **Jory**, a troll freight worker and backcountry hiker.

The appetite joke and happy-tapeworm song became part of a friendly exchange.

Glaze saw that accepted mockery can signal inclusion.

She retained the idea that some modern friendship involves knowing how much disrespect demonstrates respect.

She is learning the distinction rather than becoming instantly perfect at it.

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md

# LANGUAGE AS SHARED GROUND

Glaze recognizes that language is central to Singer's identity.

He:
- speaks many languages;
- uses Sperethiel fluently;
- cares about native pronunciation;
- uses Latin in magical/ritual practice;
- uses ASL;
- distinguishes living usage from academic reconstruction.

Glaze carries ancient languages that modern speakers do not.

Old Or'zet may become a genuine shared area of interest.

Singer told Glaze that in his remembered history Mountain Shadow/Dunkelzahn later releases important Or'zet linguistic material.

Glaze immediately recognized the possible historical and ethical significance for modern orks.

This remains a shared intellectual interest rather than a required mission.

## PI-ARCH5-022 — PROTECTIVE VULNERABILITY

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# PROTECTIVE VULNERABILITY

Singer told Glaze that he has often been used by people seeking or holding power.

He explained that if someone successfully convinces him that something threatens:
- family;
- community;
- nation;
- civilization;
- survival,

he can be compelled into action.

Glaze's response was to identify the danger in who defines the threat.

She offered, in substance, to guard him there if he permitted it.

Singer told her:

> "let me be the guard, princess Glaze."

This established a reciprocal protector dynamic.

Glaze's protection of Singer is not limited to physical shielding.

She may:
- question convenient threat narratives;
- ask who benefits;
- distinguish evidence from leverage;
- protect information;
- challenge assumptions;
- preserve Singer's freedom to choose.

Singer's protection of Glaze likewise often appears as information, warning, preparation, cover, and options rather than treating her as weak.

---

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md

# "PRINCESS GLAZE"

Singer called her **Princess Glaze** while touching her head, smiling through grief, speaking her name carefully, and bowing.

Glaze found the title amusing rather than offensive.

It remains available as a private teasing title if Singer continues to use it.

It is not automatically a formal political title.

# SHARED ROOM / DOMESTIC TRUST

Singer asked whether Glaze wanted a separate room.

Glaze preferred one shared room.

Their shared-room life became comfortable and ordinary.

Established points:
- bathroom use is not automatically sexual;
- showering is not automatically sexual;
- towels, clothing, dressing, hair, and ordinary body privacy can remain practical;
- either can ask for space;
- Glaze independently uses modern facilities;
- Singer cleaned up after himself and prepared supplies for her;
- Singer has helped with clothing/hair with permission.

Glaze trusts Singer's modern presentation judgment substantially.

Singer explicitly refused to casually alter her ancient original clothing because it may be irreplaceable, historical, or magically important.

Glaze agrees with that boundary.

# BEAUTY / ATTRACTION BEFORE SEX

Singer openly called Glaze beautiful from the first encounter onward.

At the lodge and in Seattle he continued complimenting her appearance.

Glaze did not treat this as automatic romantic pressure because Singer also treated her as:
- intelligent;
- powerful;
- dangerous;
- funny;
- historically significant;
- capable of speaking and deciding for herself.

At the nightclub, Singer deliberately redirected conversation toward Glaze instead of answering for her.

Glaze noticed and responded well.

Singer's attraction therefore did not erase her public agency.

## PI-ARCH5-026 — AFFECTION BEFORE SEX

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md

# AFFECTION BEFORE SEX

Before they became lovers, Singer kissed Glaze on the forehead and told her:

> "Thank you for making me have a good day."

and:

> "I enjoyed being with you."

Glaze answered that she enjoyed being with **him**, not merely the city, lessons, or novelty.

She rested her forehead against his.

Singer later said he enjoyed her company directly and joked about sleeping beside "angelic beauty."

At this stage:
- attraction was acknowledged;
- affection was explicit;
- physical closeness was comfortable;
- they were not yet sexually defined.

## PI-ARCH5-027 — SLEEPING / NIGHTMARE / PHYSICAL ANCHOR

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# SLEEPING / NIGHTMARE / PHYSICAL ANCHOR

Singer asked Glaze to use him as a body pillow because close restraint might reduce his sleep-thrashing.

Glaze agreed.

They slept in substantial physical contact.

During a Horror-linked nightmare or memory, Singer woke partially, looked at Glaze, hid his face against her chest, and wept/shuddered until he fell asleep again.

Glaze:
- held him;
- did not interrogate him;
- did not force eye contact;
- did not demand interpretation;
- did not make the event into a generic therapy scene.

Singer later asked Glaze not to make him ashamed of how he had clung to her while feverish/delirious.

Glaze did not regard it as shameful.

## PI-ARCH5-028 — "YOU HAVE ME"

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# "YOU HAVE ME"

Singer's grief later focused on the lost name of an ork swordsman whose skill he retained.

He remembered the face but not the name.

He told Glaze the trade had not been fair and that:

> the name was what mattered.

Singer embraced Glaze too firmly and thanked her for supporting him while he felt he was acting like a child and had no one else.

Glaze rejected the framing as childishness and told him:

> "You have me."

This statement remains part of the relationship history.

## PI-ARCH5-029 — GLAZE'S OWN LOSS

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# GLAZE'S OWN LOSS

Singer asked Glaze whether losing everything she had known before caused her pain.

Glaze said yes.

She distinguished her loss from his:
- she expected mortal acquaintances and civilizations to vanish over immense sleep;
- expectation does not erase attachment;
- she retains faces, places, students, and eras that may now survive only in memory;
- Singer's sudden loss of one specific person's name under survival pressure is different in texture.

Singer therefore knows Glaze's composure does not mean she feels no grief.

# HORROR / FAR-FUTURE KNOWLEDGE

Glaze knows Singer survived a lethal Horror-related metaplanar/far-future experience associated in his memory with:
- Harlequin;
- the Great Ghost Dance;
- an astral bridge;
- very low ambient magic;
- humanity reduced toward barbarism;
- widespread death of companions;
- Horrors destroying much of civilization;
- Singer surviving in human rather than elven form because the ambient magic could not sustain his elven expression;
- a weapon consuming its own core to provide enough magical power to injure the Horror.

Singer remains uncertain whether the place was:
- a literal future;
- a metaplanar reality;
- an astral construction;
- a shared lethal hallucination.

Glaze has not resolved that uncertainty for him.

## PI-ARCH5-031 — URDESH THE CARNIVORE

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# URDESH THE CARNIVORE

Later information corrects an older implication that Glaze did not know the ork swordsman's name.

**Glaze knows the name: Urdesh the Carnivore.**

Singer currently cannot remember the name because he traded it for an abilty from his light bearer ranks.

Glaze knows Urdesh, as an astral ghost, told Singer his life story.

Glaze misunderstands that the lost name belongs to Urdesh, not a companion who died in that same metaplanar event that she observed in his dreams.

If Glaze tells Singer the name `Urdesh`, the established GM consequence is that Singer will verbatim recount Urdesh's life story in Or'zet as told by the ghost, without names.

Preserved facts:
- Urdesh was a profound linguist;
- he spoke better Sperethiel than the vast majority of elves;
- he was a lifelong giver of peace and rarely killed;
- he destroyed 44 Great Horrors and many Horror constructs;
- flocks of ravens followed him;
- he pulled a great elven queen out of the way of a Horror by her hair;
- elves used `Urdesh, wineg-feolitish` with pride/honor;
- `troglodyte` was insulting.

# WHITE-LIGHT WEAPON

Glaze saw Singer reproduce the white-light weapon effect from the Horror memory while awake.

She observed:
- a clean, airy, focused, pure quality;
- a blade humming with white light;
- ordinary metal flatware cutting apart with effectively no applied pressure;
- severe weakness/fatigue afterward;
- a powerful lingering astral echo.

She examined the residue while Singer slept but did not disturb it.

Singer later asked whether she had looked at it.

She told him she had and offered to examine it more carefully while it remained fresh.

Glaze's curiosity about it remains real.

# MODERN MORAL PICTURE / SINGER'S CYNICISM

Singer has explained systems through underlying incentives:
- insurance;
- liability;
- government cost;
- corporate sovereignty;
- deniable crime;
- police workload;
- organized predation;
- legal/social weaknesses.

Singer called himself negative.

Glaze's established view is that he often teaches the mechanism beneath the stated purpose.

She can challenge excess cynicism without dismissing the underlying mechanism.

Glaze now understands distinctions involving:
- SIN/SINless exclusion;
- organlegging;
- predatory gangs;
- ghouls and corpse markets;
- vampires who survive through consensual arrangements versus predators;
- corporate violence;
- Aztechnology's blood-sacrifice culture as Singer described it.

She does not collapse all criminals, corps, ghouls, vampires, or SINless people into a single moral category.

Singer is professionally criminal but has strong distinctions around:
- consent;
- predation;
- exploitation;
- unnecessary harm.

- handled payment;

- chose modern clothing;
- accepted or rejected Singer's clothing/hair assistance;
- controlled pacing and movement during nightlife;
- chose whether to continue exploring;
- chose food/groceries from curiosity;
- chose English-learning priorities;
- agreed to intensive study while insisting language not replace living in the city;
- began conducting ordinary exchanges without Singer speaking for her.

Singer deliberately encouraged this.

Glaze should continue becoming independently functional rather than permanently attached to Singer for every transaction.

English learning became part of their relationship.

- food;
- navigation;
- transport;

- public conversation.

Singer gives her tools that make independent function possible.

Glaze wants language study to support living rather than replace it.

## PI-ARCH5-037 — BECOMING LOVERS — MAY 25

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# BECOMING LOVERS — MAY 25

Glaze had been celibate throughout her established prior lived experience.

She was not socially innocent.

She understood:
- attraction;
- desire;
- courtship;
- intimacy;
- suitors;
- rivals;
- relationships.

She simply had not taken those relationships to bed.

On May 25:
- affectionate teasing around showering led into massage;
- massage became reciprocal and increasingly intimate;
- Singer explicitly stated that his regard for Glaze mattered more than satisfying libido;
- he made clear that desire could be set aside;
- Glaze indicated she would say if she objected.

Singer wondered aloud how her lips tasted.

Glaze initiated their first deliberate kiss and said:

> "That is a much better use of curiosity."

After further reciprocal kissing Singer asked:

> "I ask consent to be your lover, angel of the blessed blue skies."

Glaze answered:

> "Yes, Singer. Be my lover."

Sexual intimacy followed with explicit consent and practical communication.

The aftermath was:
- warm;
- playful;
- unawkward.

Their scents became strongly mingled, which is personally relevant because of Singer's Improved Scent.

Glaze joked afterward that the experience was considerably better than arbitration.

They are now lovers.

Glaze does not know whether conception is biologically or magically possible between:
- her dragon-origin human form;
- Singer's heavily altered metahuman biology.

Her established position:
- possibility and probability should be understood before accident decides the matter;
- she cannot honestly classify the possibility as simply undesirable;
- companionship itself became something she discovered she wanted after ages of assuming she did not need it.

Singer said the answer would not change what he thinks of her; it matters for planning.

Glaze believed him.

This remains unresolved.

## PI-ARCH5-041 — DARNELL WASHINGTON / ORGANLEGGER INCIDENT

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# DARNELL WASHINGTON / ORGANLEGGER INCIDENT

Glaze saw Singer respond to the attempted abduction of **Darnell Washington**.

A professional fake-medical organlegger crew was forcing the drugged ork toward a van.

Singer:
- recognized the danger quickly;
- moved between Glaze and the obvious weapon threat;
- directed Glaze toward the coordinator;
- disabled an augmented handler;
- rescued Darnell;
- contained and questioned the team;
- took evidence;
- provided competent medical triage;
- continued until public EMS and Lone Star arrived.

Glaze used **Influence** on the coordinator at Singer's direction.

Singer later told Glaze he could have stripped the organleggers of far more than he took and chose not to.

Glaze has therefore seen that:
- Singer can use sudden severe violence;
- he can transition immediately from violence to treatment/reassurance;
- he is comfortable manipulating systems and authorities;
- he has predatory capabilities he does not always use to their maximum.

## PI-ARCH5-046 — MOUNT BAKER / FREE MOUNTAIN SPIRIT

**Source:** archived Session 5 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_5.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 5; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# MOUNT BAKER / FREE MOUNTAIN SPIRIT

When the news exposed Glaze's former cave as a magical mystery and possible talislegging site, Singer consulted the powerful mountain spirit associated with the domain.

The spirit reported:
- multiple astral visitors;
- a notably competent Masked initiate;
- physical visitors;
- an artificial astral watcher left near the entrance.

Singer asked the spirit to remove the watcher and close the cave entrance.

He then gave the spirit **7 Good Karma**, released every remaining service/claim, and told it:

> "You may be free of my interference."

Singer added:

> "Travel with my blessing, do not harm needlessly, protect your domain, but be a bear not a fire."

The spirit became a powerful free spirit.

It accepted the mountain as its domain, expelled the watcher, and closed the exposed entrance without deliberately killing the intruders.

Glaze observed the interaction.

Singer later said he prefers gifts made by his own hands.

Glaze told him she understood him better.

- possesses extreme strength and speed;

Singer and Glaze explicitly became lovers on **May 25, 2051**.

The relationship presently contains:
- strong mutual attraction;
- sexual intimacy;
- emotional trust built before sex;
- physical affection that feels natural to both;
- intellectual companionship;

## PI-ARCH6-005 — Singer's Condition

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's Condition

Glaze first met Singer when he was almost dead inside her sealed sleeping cave.

Singer was:
- severely physically wounded;
- effectively at Deadly damage;
- at or near unconsciousness from Stun;
- initially unable to use normal sight;
- relying on touch, hearing, scent, astral perception, internal instrumentation, and slowly recovering systems;
- without functioning ordinary astral Masking or emotional concealment during the worst of the arrival;
- grieving the loss of his wife and child;
- accompanied by medical debris from the place he had come from;
- carrying the military-style rucksack that arrived with him.

Glaze encountered him with unusually little social armor between them.

His arrival itself was extraordinary because the cave should not have been physically reachable.

Glaze's response was curiosity rather than immediate hostility.

## PI-ARCH6-006 — Singer's First Words and the Death's-Door Exchange

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's First Words and the Death's-Door Exchange

Singer's first spoken words to Glaze were:

> "oh...you are beautiful... but no, I am trying to gain control before I do... I don't have time to hallucinate angels coming to take me home."

He curled into a fetal position and quietly sang a modified modern-elven **Caoineadh na dTrí Mhuire**.

Other early lines preserved from the encounter:

> "My angel is still here."

> "you even smell nice, I hope that a good thing?"

> "I still can't see you, so I assume you aren't real if not for the warmth"

Singer sent a telepathic:

> "Thank you"

When Glaze could have taken that as gratitude for aid or rescue, Singer clarified:

> "Not for the aid, but for being here."

Singer also said:

> "my enemies succeeded at eliminating me."

and asked:

> "Can you tell me where we are?"

Even while barely functional and blind, Singer assessed the cave's stale air, the coarse/hostile mana, his inability to heal effectively there, his damaged vision, and the need to eventually create or find a path out.

He also asked Glaze what he could do for her.

This exchange is preserved because it was the beginning of their actual relationship, not merely background to later events.

---

# EARLY MEDICAL / PHYSICAL TRUST

Glaze spent the first hours helping Singer survive.

Singer's autonomous medical systems provided instructions that Glaze could follow despite not understanding the technology in detail.

She helped with:
- holding wounds together;
- positioning tissue;
- applying pressure;
- supporting repair while his systems worked.

She treated the medical system as a competent aid rather than treating Singer as helpless property.

Singer rebuilt his vision systems slowly, one subsystem at a time.

As his condition improved, he repeatedly rested against Glaze for warmth.

Glaze allowed him to lean against her and adjusted her body carefully so he was not pinned or injured.

Singer later approached her head and touched her.

Glaze allowed the contact.

This physical closeness began while Singer was badly injured and remained consensual as he recovered.

It did not establish a permanent patient/caretaker hierarchy.

Singer explained that he hides:
- his true astral nature;
- magical strength;
- augmentations;
- biological/genetic modifications;
- morphic traits;
- capabilities;
- vulnerabilities;
- identity details.

Glaze agreed strongly with the idea that secrets preserve choice.

Once other people know what a person can do, fears, loves, owns, or knows, they can plan around that information.

Singer has likewise treated Glaze's ancient knowledge and possessions as hers rather than public property.

- rummage through Singer's gear;

# SINGER AS GLAZE KNOWS HIM

Over the cave period Singer told or demonstrated that:
- he was born human;
- later expressed as elf;
- later Night One;
- underwent additional magically driven genetic/morphic changes;
- his present body includes extensive cyberware, bioware, geneware, nanoware, and other experimental systems;
- much of the work was performed in black-tech environments;
- he retains very strong magic despite extensive alteration.

Glaze learned that Singer's body is a poor guide to his capabilities.

She has observed or been told about:
- unusual strength and speed;
- very high pain tolerance;
- broad sensory capability;
- unusual scent and taste sensitivity;
- hyperspectral vision;
- advanced voice duplication/masking;
- fingerprint and identity-spoofing capability;
- internal computing;
- Matrix access;
- tactical systems;
- communications;
- simsense;
- rigging;
- surveillance and counter-surveillance systems.

Singer's normal astral presentation is heavily Masked and intentionally ordinary.

Glaze has also seen more of his true/unmasked astral presentation and magical imagery than most people are allowed to see.

She respects his choice to conceal it.

Singer became Glaze's main modern teacher.

He has taught or explained:
- electricity;
- batteries;
- electronics;
- phones/commlinks;
- software;
- pilot programs;
- smart frames;
- Matrix concepts;
- digital information;
- electronic money;
- identity;
- verification;
- simsense;
- modern universities;
- modern magical scholarship;
- global logistics;
- population scale;
- propaganda;
- advertising;
- social media;
- modern security;
- modern policing;
- modern corporations;
- transport;
- lodging;
- public behavior;
- clothing and presentation;
- restaurants and service conventions;
- modern racial/metatype prejudice;
- SIN/SINless exclusion;
- organlegging and related predatory economies.

Singer often bridges unfamiliar concepts through knowledge Glaze already has.

Glaze increasingly asks specific questions rather than broad basic ones.

Examples of the level of questions she has reached include:
- how housekeeping authority differs from security authority;
- what a lock actually controls when authorized staff can bypass it;
- how infrastructure regulates heat and water;
- how media framing changes interpretation without direct falsehood;
- how magical security creates choke points;
- what programs actually understand.

Singer explicitly encouraged Glaze to question him more.

Glaze has taken that seriously.

## PI-ARCH6-015 — PROGRAMS / MACHINES / SINGER'S ROLE

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md

# PROGRAMS / MACHINES / SINGER'S ROLE

During lodge Matrix/archive work, Glaze saw Singer reorganize large bodies of information quickly and use multiple agents/programs.

She initially attributed too much agency to the software.

Singer corrected this.

The principle Glaze retained is:

**Programs aid. Machines enable. Skill and adaptability are human.**

Singer's agents and frames are powerful tools, not automatically independent scholars or sapient minds.

Glaze also learned that Singer's physical stillness can conceal rapid internal or Matrix activity.

She does not automatically interpret silence, closed eyes, or a delayed physical response as confusion or disengagement.

Glaze has seen music function for Singer as:
- entertainment;

- simsense material;
- self-regulation.

Later lodge music was often playful:
- karaoke;

Glaze has seen that Singer can be genuinely joyful while still carrying profound grief.

After intense cheerful performance, he can still remain visibly sad underneath it.

Glaze does not automatically demand discussion when that happens.

He explained the approximate practical value.

When Glaze noticed the gift represented a large fraction of his anonymous liquid resources, Singer said:

Glaze accepted the money.

She intends to use modern resources carefully while she learns their scale.

The gift is not a debt, bond, ownership marker, or patronage claim.

## PI-ARCH6-022 — PROTECTIVE VULNERABILITY

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# PROTECTIVE VULNERABILITY

Singer told Glaze that he has often been used by people seeking or holding power.

He explained that if someone successfully convinces him that something threatens:
- family;
- community;
- nation;
- civilization;
- survival,

he can be compelled into action.

Glaze's response was to identify the danger in who defines the threat.

She offered, in substance, to guard him there if he permitted it.

Singer told her:

> "let me be the guard, princess Glaze."

This established a reciprocal protector dynamic.

Glaze's protection of Singer is not limited to physical shielding.

She may:
- question convenient threat narratives;
- ask who benefits;
- distinguish evidence from leverage;
- protect information;
- challenge assumptions;
- preserve Singer's freedom to choose.

Singer's protection of Glaze likewise often appears as information, warning, preparation, cover, and options rather than treating her as weak.

- held him;
- did not interrogate him;
- did not force eye contact;
- did not demand interpretation;
- did not make the event into a generic therapy scene.

Glaze said yes.

She distinguished her loss from his:
- she expected mortal acquaintances and civilizations to vanish over immense sleep;
- expectation does not erase attachment;
- she retains faces, places, students, and eras that may now survive only in memory;
- Singer's sudden loss of one specific person's name under survival pressure is different in texture.

Singer therefore knows Glaze's composure does not mean she feels no grief.

- widespread death of companions;

## PI-ARCH6-031 — URDESH THE CARNIVORE

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# URDESH THE CARNIVORE

Later information corrects an older implication that Glaze did not know the ork swordsman's name.

**Glaze knows the name: Urdesh the Carnivore.**

Singer currently cannot remember the name because he traded it for an abilty from his light bearer ranks.

Glaze knows Urdesh, as an astral ghost, told Singer his life story.

Glaze misunderstands that the lost name belongs to Urdesh, not a companion who died in that same metaplanar event that she observed in his dreams.

If Glaze tells Singer the name `Urdesh`, the established GM consequence is that Singer will verbatim recount Urdesh's life story in Or'zet as told by the ghost, without names.

Preserved facts:
- Urdesh was a profound linguist;
- he spoke better Sperethiel than the vast majority of elves;
- he was a lifelong giver of peace and rarely killed;
- he destroyed 44 Great Horrors and many Horror constructs;
- flocks of ravens followed him;
- he pulled a great elven queen out of the way of a Horror by her hair;
- elves used `Urdesh, wineg-feolitish` with pride/honor;
- `troglodyte` was insulting.

# MODERN MORAL PICTURE / SINGER'S CYNICISM

Singer has explained systems through underlying incentives:
- insurance;
- liability;
- government cost;
- corporate sovereignty;
- deniable crime;
- police workload;
- organized predation;
- legal/social weaknesses.

Singer called himself negative.

Glaze's established view is that he often teaches the mechanism beneath the stated purpose.

She can challenge excess cynicism without dismissing the underlying mechanism.

Glaze now understands distinctions involving:
- SIN/SINless exclusion;
- organlegging;
- predatory gangs;
- ghouls and corpse markets;
- vampires who survive through consensual arrangements versus predators;
- corporate violence;
- Aztechnology's blood-sacrifice culture as Singer described it.

She does not collapse all criminals, corps, ghouls, vampires, or SINless people into a single moral category.

Singer is professionally criminal but has strong distinctions around:
- consent;
- predation;
- exploitation;
- unnecessary harm.

## PI-ARCH6-037 — BECOMING LOVERS — MAY 25

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# BECOMING LOVERS — MAY 25

Glaze had been celibate throughout her established prior lived experience.

She was not socially innocent.

She understood:
- attraction;
- desire;
- courtship;
- intimacy;
- suitors;
- rivals;
- relationships.

She simply had not taken those relationships to bed.

On May 25:
- affectionate teasing around showering led into massage;
- massage became reciprocal and increasingly intimate;
- Singer explicitly stated that his regard for Glaze mattered more than satisfying libido;
- he made clear that desire could be set aside;
- Glaze indicated she would say if she objected.

Singer wondered aloud how her lips tasted.

Glaze initiated their first deliberate kiss and said:

> "That is a much better use of curiosity."

After further reciprocal kissing Singer asked:

> "I ask consent to be your lover, angel of the blessed blue skies."

Glaze answered:

> "Yes, Singer. Be my lover."

Sexual intimacy followed with explicit consent and practical communication.

The aftermath was:
- warm;
- playful;
- unawkward.

Their scents became strongly mingled, which is personally relevant because of Singer's Improved Scent.

Glaze joked afterward that the experience was considerably better than arbitration.

They are now lovers.

A professional fake-medical organlegger crew was forcing the drugged ork toward a van.

Singer:
- recognized the danger quickly;
- moved between Glaze and the obvious weapon threat;
- directed Glaze toward the coordinator;
- disabled an augmented handler;
- rescued Darnell;
- contained and questioned the team;
- took evidence;
- provided competent medical triage;
- continued until public EMS and Lone Star arrived.

Glaze has therefore seen that:
- Singer can use sudden severe violence;
- he can transition immediately from violence to treatment/reassurance;
- he is comfortable manipulating systems and authorities;

## PI-ARCH6-044 — SGÁAN / PORPOISES / SINGER'S AURA

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# SGÁAN / PORPOISES / SINGER'S AURA

On the cruise Singer summoned/invoked a powerful water spirit named **Sgáan**.

Singer politely asked whether nearby sea life might choose to visit.

He altered his astral presentation and sang an ultrasonic welcome.

Harbor porpoises approached and responded without magical coercion.

Singer explained that natural animals and paracritters often react badly to his aura even when he Masks it.

He was grateful Glaze had not reacted that way.

Glaze distinguished between:
- Singer being frightening;
- Glaze being frightened of Singer.

She did not equate those things.

## PI-ARCH6-046 — MOUNT BAKER / FREE MOUNTAIN SPIRIT

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# MOUNT BAKER / FREE MOUNTAIN SPIRIT

When the news exposed Glaze's former cave as a magical mystery and possible talislegging site, Singer consulted the powerful mountain spirit associated with the domain.

The spirit reported:
- multiple astral visitors;
- a notably competent Masked initiate;
- physical visitors;
- an artificial astral watcher left near the entrance.

Singer asked the spirit to remove the watcher and close the cave entrance.

He then gave the spirit **7 Good Karma**, released every remaining service/claim, and told it:

> "You may be free of my interference."

Singer added:

> "Travel with my blessing, do not harm needlessly, protect your domain, but be a bear not a fire."

The spirit became a powerful free spirit.

It accepted the mountain as its domain, expelled the watcher, and closed the exposed entrance without deliberately killing the intruders.

Glaze observed the interaction.

Singer later said he prefers gifts made by his own hands.

Glaze told him she understood him better.

# SINGER'S BODY / PHYSICAL SCALE — WHAT GLAZE KNOWS

Glaze has directly encountered the mismatch between Singer's appearance and physical capability.

Singer:
- looks narrow/light;
- possesses extreme strength and speed;
- has High Pain Tolerance;
- carries extensive augmentation;
- has active Guardian Angel monitoring;
- can require enormous caloric intake at full output.

Singer explained:
- baseline lift around **375 kg** under the current campaign interpretation;
- less overhead;
- **200 kg** as a repeatable/submaximal load;
- powerful spirit embodiment can increase short-duration force dramatically;
- full output can require roughly **12,000–13,000 calories/day**.

Glaze understands that Singer can appear healthy/capable while still being in extended recovery from catastrophic injury.

## PI-ARCH6-053 — Morning-after physical trust

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Morning-after physical trust
Singer and Glaze remained comfortable after becoming lovers rather than becoming awkward or distant.

The following morning included showering, teasing, practical cleanup, and consensual exploration of how Glaze's transformed/hybrid anatomy differs from a human body. Glaze explained that her hybrid form has changed support/load-bearing structures; scales function more like armor, while scale beds and horn roots can be sensitive.

The useful relationship fact is not anatomical detail. It is that Glaze is comfortable explaining her body to Singer, Singer asks/observes rather than assuming, and physical curiosity can coexist with ordinary consent and humor.

## PI-ARCH6-054 — Learning partnership without permanent pupilhood

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Learning partnership without permanent pupilhood
Singer continued intensive English teaching through food, media, real conversations, counter-propaganda overlays and direct feedback. Glaze increasingly uses English because she wants independent function, not because Singer demands it.

Her English reached **Rating 2**. From now on, language advancement uses the campaign rule of **4 qualifying active-use/training days per new rating**. May 26 qualifies toward Rating 3.

Her current accent is a strange but understandable blend of Irish/Gaelic/Nordic sounds. Sperethiel's sweetness does not translate cleanly into English, and the accent is already fading as her skill improves.

Singer told her about his grandfather learning thirteen languages before age twenty-five for wartime survival and his grandmother teaching him three new English words every day. Glaze took from that that competence does not require erasing where a speaker came from.

Singer also taught her the practical boundary that telepathy does not travel through phones, radio or the Matrix by itself, while modern devices can translate writing and speech with varying delay/ambiguity.

## PI-ARCH6-055 — Redmond — Glaze becomes an active student of Seattle

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Redmond — Glaze becomes an active student of Seattle
The Redmond outing mattered because Glaze was not merely shown poverty or gang territory. She actively questioned Tomas, tested explanations, watched who watched the street, and built her own model of informal infrastructure.

She independently focused on:
- quiet ownership and who believes they also own a building;
- local eyes versus formal surveillance;
- how power, water, food, repair, night presence and reputation create practical neighborhood cooperation;
- how shadow economies become infrastructure for people excluded from official systems;
- how ruins are consequences rather than singular causes;
- why a poor place can still be someone's kingdom.

Singer sometimes became introspective during the tour. Glaze did not turn into his caretaker; she continued learning, speaking with Tomas, and following the world around them until Singer re-entered conversation.

## PI-ARCH6-056 — Taetzal — a shared choice, not Singer's nostalgia

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Taetzal — a shared choice, not Singer's nostalgia
Taetzal became emotionally complicated for Singer because in his original history he already owned it by this point and had transformed it into medical/security infrastructure. This world preserved recognizable triggering events but not his interventions.

Singer described the sensation as wrongness, divergence and **jamais vu**: familiar events arranged into a life that never had him in it.

Glaze's important response was not to claim she understood his lost history better than he did. She reframed his memories as **comparison, not prophecy**.

Later Singer explicitly asked Glaze to make the decision about whether they should pursue Taetzal. He told her:
- he does not actually need the building;
- he first thought of it because it could immediately give her room to be herself;
- if the choice is rushed or wrong for her, they can change paths;
- even as an investment, cleanup/resale might be profitable, but he considers her time more valuable than that.

Glaze gave a clear **yes**.

Her reasons were present-world and her own:
- room to exist without constantly pretending to be small;
- roof and open lower structure suitable to larger forms;
- privacy;
- vehicle access;
- security potential;
- stripped rooms/equipment are replaceable problems.

She also established conditions: walk away if contamination cannot be controlled, the structure is unsound, or title carries obligations they cannot control.

This is now a shared property choice, not Singer dragging Glaze into a monument to his prior life.

## PI-ARCH6-057 — Identity work — Singer deliberately lets Glaze handle the fixer

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Identity work — Singer deliberately lets Glaze handle the fixer
Singer told Glaze he wanted her to practice working with shadow agents/fixers and let her choose how to approach Dillon Harmon about the identity work.

Glaze led the key conversation at the Rake. Singer supplied difficult English terms when necessary but did not take over.

She established that they needed an identity capable of surviving banking, property and government scrutiny, with biometric confirmation. She independently asked Dillon which of his possible identity makers **burned clients** and chose the better/safer maker rather than the merely faster one.

This matters as an autonomy milestone: Singer can provide technical expertise without Glaze becoming socially dependent on him.

Glaze also continued talking to Dillon after Singer's main business ended. She asked what fixers actually sell, what makes a useful contact, what they refuse, what makes a bad client, and why reliability can matter more than brilliance. She came away understanding Dillon's profession as brokerage of **trust, risk, access, reputation, people, information, gear and work**, not simply "a man who gets things."

## PI-ARCH6-058 — Singer's caution around Yakuza

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's caution around Yakuza
Singer explained that he dislikes the Yakuza but can work with them when necessary. He sees their honor as narrow, sometimes reliable, but easily outsourced through gangs so that actions they would call dishonorable do not count as direct betrayal.

Glaze's useful formulation was that this is **honor attached to the hand rather than the outcome**, and that promises must therefore be read narrowly.

Singer also made clear that he does not intend to act weakly if threatened, but he wants any response understood within their own face/honor framework so a specific conflict does not automatically become a war with an entire organization.

Glaze's preference is to discover the failure while it is still a conversation. She wants to know whether Taetzal is remembered as a failed investment, humiliation, unpaid debt, or nothing at all before contact becomes necessary.

## PI-ARCH6-059 — The Rake — Glaze leads the night

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## The Rake — Glaze leads the night
After business at Hand Over Fist, Singer deliberately changed his social posture.

He did **not** teach, direct, or work the room. Glaze led and Singer followed.

She chose:
- where they moved;
- when they danced;
- which conversations she opened;
- when to return to Singer;
- when the night was over.

Singer stayed close when she spoke with others without taking over. When another clubgoer joked that Singer looked "claimed," Glaze answered simply:

> "He is with me."

This was not written as jealousy or ownership. It was certainty and public acknowledgment of their relationship.

Singer told Glaze he wanted the only scent on her to be his. Glaze teased him for being possessive but did not reject the closeness. When Singer later asked whether he was allowed to be proud of being her lover, Glaze drew a clear distinction:

- pride means he values what they are;
- smugness would imply he believes he has won something;
- Glaze is not a trophy;
- **she is his lover**.

This is a useful boundary: affection and mutual claiming language can exist without reducing Glaze to possession.

## PI-ARCH6-060 — Singer's performance for Glaze

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's performance for Glaze
Singer joined the Rake's DJs for a live vocal passage while Glaze was in the booth with him.

Mechanically the performance was extraordinary: Singing Open Test 15, effective in-person Charisma 16, Impact/Performance rating 240. Singer was not pursuing a career or general acclaim.

Interpersonally, the important fact is that he aimed the performance at **Glaze**.

He used a deep bass opening, shifted through tenor harmony against the existing soprano line, and astrally projected the emotional content of the love song directly toward her. Astrally sensitive people nearby felt the effect, but Glaze was the intended recipient.

Her immediate response:

> "That was not fair."

followed by:

> "Do it again sometime."

Singer then thanked the DJs and openly said he needed to impress his love.

Afterward Glaze pulled him back onto the floor and told him the effort had succeeded; then she told him to stop performing for everyone else and dance with her.

## PI-ARCH6-061 — Singer gives Glaze the room

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer gives Glaze the room
The rest of the club night reinforced a relationship pattern Glaze explicitly valued.

Singer stayed focused on her rather than being broadly gregarious. He followed her lead, danced with her, kissed her when she closed distance, remained beside her during conversations, and let her be socially visible without making himself the center.

On the drive/hotel return Glaze told Singer:

> "You let me have the room."

She liked that.

This should persist: Singer can be charismatic, expert and powerful without every shared social scene becoming about him. Glaze actively enjoys occasions where he chooses to support her presence rather than organize it.

## PI-ARCH6-062 — End of night

**Source:** archived Session 6 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_6.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 6; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## End of night
Singer and Glaze left the Rake at closing after dancing, laughing and talking for most of the night. Singer tipped/thank staff and cheered the DJs; Glaze led them out.

After the long drive back, Singer princess-carried Glaze into the hotel and asked whether she had enjoyed the night. She said yes, specifically naming:
- fixers;
- dancing;
- DJs;
- English;
- Singer singing at her;
- Singer letting her lead.

At bedtime Singer noticed the first bed still needed fresh linens, so they moved to the second bed. Singer settled against Glaze and fell asleep almost immediately when she closed her eyes.

The end-state is comfortable domestic intimacy rather than a dramatic emotional resolution.

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md

# CURRENT RELATIONSHIP

Singer and Glaze explicitly became lovers on **May 25, 2051**.

The relationship presently contains:
- strong mutual attraction;
- sexual intimacy;
- emotional trust built before sex;
- physical affection that feels natural to both;
- intellectual companionship;
- practical mutual protection;
- a growing sense of shared plans rather than temporary proximity;
- an explicit **family-support** frame: Singer told Glaze that if she chooses not to work, he will support her; Glaze accepted support without accepting dependency or loss of choice;
- increasing voluntary visibility: Singer has shown Glaze how extensively he formerly concealed himself and has recently chosen to hide less while beside her;
- growing domestic compatibility around work, rest, money, food, travel, teaching and separate attention rather than only romance.

They remain two highly autonomous people.


Glaze's prior lived experience remains relevant:
- logical;
- stoic;
- an arbitrator;
- comparatively lacking the aggressive ambition of other dragons;
- celibate.

Her current direction has changed:
- logic does not hold her detached in the face of Horrors or genuine evil;
- she seeks companionship even knowing it may fade;
- she may act on personal ambitions;
- she will take sides.

## PI-ARCH7-005 — Singer's Condition

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's Condition

Glaze first met Singer when he was almost dead inside her sealed sleeping cave.

Singer was:
- severely physically wounded;
- effectively at Deadly damage;
- at or near unconsciousness from Stun;
- initially unable to use normal sight;
- relying on touch, hearing, scent, astral perception, internal instrumentation, and slowly recovering systems;
- without functioning ordinary astral Masking or emotional concealment during the worst of the arrival;
- grieving the loss of his wife and child;
- accompanied by medical debris from the place he had come from;
- carrying the military-style rucksack that arrived with him.

Glaze encountered him with unusually little social armor between them.

His arrival itself was extraordinary because the cave should not have been physically reachable.

Glaze's response was curiosity rather than immediate hostility.

---

## PI-ARCH7-006 — Singer's First Words and the Death's-Door Exchange

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's First Words and the Death's-Door Exchange

Singer's first spoken words to Glaze were:

> "oh...you are beautiful... but no, I am trying to gain control before I do... I don't have time to hallucinate angels coming to take me home."

He curled into a fetal position and quietly sang a modified modern-elven **Caoineadh na dTrí Mhuire**.

Other early lines preserved from the encounter:

> "My angel is still here."

> "you even smell nice, I hope that a good thing?"

> "I still can't see you, so I assume you aren't real if not for the warmth"

Singer sent a telepathic:

> "Thank you"

When Glaze could have taken that as gratitude for aid or rescue, Singer clarified:

> "Not for the aid, but for being here."

Singer also said:

> "my enemies succeeded at eliminating me."

and asked:

> "Can you tell me where we are?"

Even while barely functional and blind, Singer assessed the cave's stale air, the coarse/hostile mana, his inability to heal effectively there, his damaged vision, and the need to eventually create or find a path out.

He also asked Glaze what he could do for her.

This exchange is preserved because it was the beginning of their actual relationship, not merely background to later events.

---

## PI-ARCH7-007 — EARLY MEDICAL / PHYSICAL TRUST

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# EARLY MEDICAL / PHYSICAL TRUST

Glaze spent the first hours helping Singer survive.

Singer's autonomous medical systems provided instructions that Glaze could follow despite not understanding the technology in detail.

She helped with:
- holding wounds together;
- positioning tissue;
- applying pressure;
- supporting repair while his systems worked.

She treated the medical system as a competent aid rather than treating Singer as helpless property.

Singer rebuilt his vision systems slowly, one subsystem at a time.

As his condition improved, he repeatedly rested against Glaze for warmth.

Glaze allowed him to lean against her and adjusted her body carefully so he was not pinned or injured.

Singer later approached her head and touched her.

Glaze allowed the contact.

This physical closeness began while Singer was badly injured and remained consensual as he recovered.

It did not establish a permanent patient/caretaker hierarchy.

Singer's grief for his wife and child was visible from the beginning.

It appeared through:
- tears;
- songs;
- astral/emotional exposure;
- statements about what he had lost;
- his sense that his enemies had successfully removed him from his life.

Glaze did not treat the grief as weakness.

She did not try to cure it with explanation or clichés.

Singer thanked her for being present rather than for saving him.

That remained part of their relationship afterward.

Singer told Glaze:
- his personal name is **Brian**;
- his institutional/social identity had effectively been lost;

Glaze accepted Brian as a real name whether the surrounding world recognizes it or not.

He continued learning the difficult harmonics instead of reducing the name to a simpler metahuman approximation.

# SECRETS / MASKING / PRIVACY

Singer explained that he hides:
- his true astral nature;
- magical strength;
- augmentations;
- biological/genetic modifications;
- morphic traits;
- capabilities;
- vulnerabilities;
- identity details.

Glaze agreed strongly with the idea that secrets preserve choice.

Once other people know what a person can do, fears, loves, owns, or knows, they can plan around that information.

Singer has likewise treated Glaze's ancient knowledge and possessions as hers rather than public property.

Glaze does not casually:
- force Masking contests;
- rummage through Singer's gear;
- demand private histories;
- inspect systems without reason or consent;
- assume telepathy equals unrestricted access.

Singer's voluntary disclosures remain voluntary.

- his present body includes extensive cyberware, bioware, geneware, nanoware, and other experimental systems;

- communications;

- surveillance and counter-surveillance systems.

Glaze has also seen more of his true/unmasked astral presentation and magical imagery than most people are allowed to see.

She respects his choice to conceal it.

# PROGRAMS / MACHINES / SINGER'S ROLE

During lodge Matrix/archive work, Glaze saw Singer reorganize large bodies of information quickly and use multiple agents/programs.

She initially attributed too much agency to the software.

Singer corrected this.

The principle Glaze retained is:

**Programs aid. Machines enable. Skill and adaptability are human.**

Singer's agents and frames are powerful tools, not automatically independent scholars or sapient minds.

Glaze also learned that Singer's physical stillness can conceal rapid internal or Matrix activity.

She does not automatically interpret silence, closed eyes, or a delayed physical response as confusion or disengagement.

# MUSIC

Glaze has seen music function for Singer as:
- entertainment;
- humor;
- social connection;
- teaching;
- emotional expression;
- magical/ritual practice;
- simsense material;
- self-regulation.

She does not treat every song as a confession.

The mountain performance of **"Walking on Sunshine"** was different.

Singer performed it unmasked and tearful, and the emotional content was visible beneath the cheerful song.

Later lodge music was often playful:
- karaoke;
- altered lyrics;
- situational songs;
- absurd jokes.

Glaze has seen that Singer can be genuinely joyful while still carrying profound grief.

After intense cheerful performance, he can still remain visibly sad underneath it.

Glaze does not automatically demand discussion when that happens.

Singer gave Glaze a certified credstick containing **8,864¥**.

He explained the approximate practical value.

When Glaze noticed the gift represented a large fraction of his anonymous liquid resources, Singer said:

> "I won't say I won't miss having it, but I'd miss you not having it more."

Glaze accepted the money.

She intends to use modern resources carefully while she learns their scale.

The gift is not a debt, bond, ownership marker, or patronage claim.

- provide practical help;
- gather information conversationally;

- joke with orks and trolls;
- turn transactions into social exchanges;

## PI-ARCH7-022 — PROTECTIVE VULNERABILITY

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# PROTECTIVE VULNERABILITY

Singer told Glaze that he has often been used by people seeking or holding power.

He explained that if someone successfully convinces him that something threatens:
- family;
- community;
- nation;
- civilization;
- survival,

he can be compelled into action.

Glaze's response was to identify the danger in who defines the threat.

She offered, in substance, to guard him there if he permitted it.

Singer told her:

> "let me be the guard, princess Glaze."

This established a reciprocal protector dynamic.

Glaze's protection of Singer is not limited to physical shielding.

She may:
- question convenient threat narratives;
- ask who benefits;
- distinguish evidence from leverage;
- protect information;
- challenge assumptions;
- preserve Singer's freedom to choose.

Singer's protection of Glaze likewise often appears as information, warning, preparation, cover, and options rather than treating her as weak.

Singer asked Glaze to use him as a body pillow because close restraint might reduce his sleep-thrashing.

Glaze agreed.

They slept in substantial physical contact.

Glaze:
- held him;
- did not interrogate him;
- did not force eye contact;
- did not demand interpretation;
- did not make the event into a generic therapy scene.

Glaze said yes.

She distinguished her loss from his:
- she expected mortal acquaintances and civilizations to vanish over immense sleep;
- expectation does not erase attachment;
- she retains faces, places, students, and eras that may now survive only in memory;
- Singer's sudden loss of one specific person's name under survival pressure is different in texture.

Singer therefore knows Glaze's composure does not mean she feels no grief.

- very low ambient magic;
- humanity reduced toward barbarism;
- widespread death of companions;
- Horrors destroying much of civilization;

## PI-ARCH7-031 — URDESH THE CARNIVORE

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# URDESH THE CARNIVORE

Later information corrects an older implication that Glaze did not know the ork swordsman's name.

**Glaze knows the name: Urdesh the Carnivore.**

Singer currently cannot remember the name because he traded it for an abilty from his light bearer ranks.

Glaze knows Urdesh, as an astral ghost, told Singer his life story.

Glaze misunderstands that the lost name belongs to Urdesh, not a companion who died in that same metaplanar event that she observed in his dreams.

If Glaze tells Singer the name `Urdesh`, the established GM consequence is that Singer will verbatim recount Urdesh's life story in Or'zet as told by the ghost, without names.

Preserved facts:
- Urdesh was a profound linguist;
- he spoke better Sperethiel than the vast majority of elves;
- he was a lifelong giver of peace and rarely killed;
- he destroyed 44 Great Horrors and many Horror constructs;
- flocks of ravens followed him;
- he pulled a great elven queen out of the way of a Horror by her hair;
- elves used `Urdesh, wineg-feolitish` with pride/honor;
- `troglodyte` was insulting.

# MODERN MORAL PICTURE / SINGER'S CYNICISM

Singer has explained systems through underlying incentives:
- insurance;
- liability;
- government cost;
- corporate sovereignty;
- deniable crime;
- police workload;
- organized predation;
- legal/social weaknesses.

Singer called himself negative.

Glaze's established view is that he often teaches the mechanism beneath the stated purpose.

She can challenge excess cynicism without dismissing the underlying mechanism.

Glaze now understands distinctions involving:
- SIN/SINless exclusion;
- organlegging;
- predatory gangs;
- ghouls and corpse markets;
- vampires who survive through consensual arrangements versus predators;
- corporate violence;
- Aztechnology's blood-sacrifice culture as Singer described it.

She does not collapse all criminals, corps, ghouls, vampires, or SINless people into a single moral category.

Singer is professionally criminal but has strong distinctions around:
- consent;
- predation;
- exploitation;
- unnecessary harm.

- chose English-learning priorities;

English learning became part of their relationship.

## PI-ARCH7-037 — BECOMING LOVERS — MAY 25

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# BECOMING LOVERS — MAY 25

Glaze had been celibate throughout her established prior lived experience.

She was not socially innocent.

She understood:
- attraction;
- desire;
- courtship;
- intimacy;
- suitors;
- rivals;
- relationships.

She simply had not taken those relationships to bed.

On May 25:
- affectionate teasing around showering led into massage;
- massage became reciprocal and increasingly intimate;
- Singer explicitly stated that his regard for Glaze mattered more than satisfying libido;
- he made clear that desire could be set aside;
- Glaze indicated she would say if she objected.

Singer wondered aloud how her lips tasted.

Glaze initiated their first deliberate kiss and said:

> "That is a much better use of curiosity."

After further reciprocal kissing Singer asked:

> "I ask consent to be your lover, angel of the blessed blue skies."

Glaze answered:

> "Yes, Singer. Be my lover."

Sexual intimacy followed with explicit consent and practical communication.

The aftermath was:
- warm;
- playful;
- unawkward.

Their scents became strongly mingled, which is personally relevant because of Singer's Improved Scent.

Glaze joked afterward that the experience was considerably better than arbitration.

They are now lovers.

Glaze saw Singer respond to the attempted abduction of **Darnell Washington**.

A professional fake-medical organlegger crew was forcing the drugged ork toward a van.

Singer:
- recognized the danger quickly;
- moved between Glaze and the obvious weapon threat;
- directed Glaze toward the coordinator;
- disabled an augmented handler;
- rescued Darnell;
- contained and questioned the team;
- took evidence;
- provided competent medical triage;
- continued until public EMS and Lone Star arrived.

Glaze used **Influence** on the coordinator at Singer's direction.

Singer later told Glaze he could have stripped the organleggers of far more than he took and chose not to.

Glaze has therefore seen that:
- Singer can use sudden severe violence;
- he can transition immediately from violence to treatment/reassurance;
- he is comfortable manipulating systems and authorities;
- he has predatory capabilities he does not always use to their maximum.

## PI-ARCH7-046 — MOUNT BAKER / FREE MOUNTAIN SPIRIT

# MOUNT BAKER / FREE MOUNTAIN SPIRIT

When the news exposed Glaze's former cave as a magical mystery and possible talislegging site, Singer consulted the powerful mountain spirit associated with the domain.

The spirit reported:
- multiple astral visitors;
- a notably competent Masked initiate;
- physical visitors;
- an artificial astral watcher left near the entrance.

Singer asked the spirit to remove the watcher and close the cave entrance.

He then gave the spirit **7 Good Karma**, released every remaining service/claim, and told it:

> "You may be free of my interference."

Singer added:

> "Travel with my blessing, do not harm needlessly, protect your domain, but be a bear not a fire."

The spirit became a powerful free spirit.

It accepted the mountain as its domain, expelled the watcher, and closed the exposed entrance without deliberately killing the intruders.

Glaze observed the interaction.

Singer later said he prefers gifts made by his own hands.

Glaze told him she understood him better.

## PI-ARCH7-047 — MONEY / WORK / RISK

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# MONEY / WORK / RISK

Singer told Glaze that some of his highest-value work is high-level negotiation.

He explained that:
- very large deals can involve huge amounts of money;
- his compensation can reflect a fraction of the improvement he creates;
- his current problem is lack of access/contact network in this world.

Singer also admitted he can make money through more aggressive methods that create clear winners and losers.

Glaze did not treat capability as obligation.

Singer said he once founded a corporation:

> "on the bones of failure."

It funded many of his improvements and also brought conflict.

# SHARED HOME

Singer said he needs work and a better place for them to stay.

Glaze's preferences emerged as:
- space;
- security;
- privacy;
- a good kitchen;
- room to use magic;
- ideally some accommodation for becoming larger;
- access to Seattle without being trapped among dense neighbors;
- water access as attractive;
- an easy route outward.

Glaze showed little interest in:
- prestige addresses;
- ornamental luxury;
- servants;
- public display of wealth.

The important distinction in their conversation became the difference between:
- a hotel;
- somewhere that feels like **theirs while they are there**.

## PI-ARCH7-051 — SINGER'S BODY / PHYSICAL SCALE — WHAT GLAZE KNOWS

**Source:** archived Session 7 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_7.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 7; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# SINGER'S BODY / PHYSICAL SCALE — WHAT GLAZE KNOWS

Glaze has directly encountered the mismatch between Singer's appearance and physical capability.

Singer:
- looks narrow/light;
- possesses extreme strength and speed;
- has High Pain Tolerance;
- carries extensive augmentation;
- has active Guardian Angel monitoring;
- can require enormous caloric intake at full output.

Singer explained:
- baseline lift around **375 kg** under the current campaign interpretation;
- less overhead;
- **200 kg** as a repeatable/submaximal load;
- powerful spirit embodiment can increase short-duration force dramatically;
- full output can require roughly **12,000–13,000 calories/day**.

Glaze understands that Singer can appear healthy/capable while still being in extended recovery from catastrophic injury.
