# PART 2 — EVENT LOG

## PI-ARCH10-016 — The cave

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## The cave
Glaze first met Singer when he was almost dead inside her sealed sleeping cave, which should not have been physically reachable. He was severely wounded and near unconsciousness, unable to use normal sight, relying on touch, hearing, scent, astral perception, internal instrumentation and slowly recovering systems, without functioning astral Masking or emotional concealment during the worst of it, grieving his wife and child, surrounded by medical debris from wherever he had come from, with the military-style rucksack that arrived with him. Glaze met him with unusually little social armor between them. Her response was curiosity, not hostility.

**First words and the death's-door exchange.** He told her she was beautiful and that he did not have time to hallucinate angels, curled fetal, and quietly sang a modified modern-elven **Caoineadh na dTrí Mhuire**. Other preserved lines: "My angel is still here." / "you even smell nice, I hope that a good thing?" / "I still can't see you, so I assume you aren't real if not for the warmth." He sent a telepathic "Thank you," and when it could have read as gratitude for aid, clarified: "Not for the aid, but for being here." He said his enemies had succeeded at eliminating him and asked where they were. Even blind and barely functional he assessed the stale air, the coarse hostile mana, his inability to heal there, his damaged vision, and the eventual need for a path out. He asked what he could do for her. This is where the relationship actually began.

**Medical and physical trust.** Glaze spent the first hours helping him survive, following his medical system's instructions without understanding the technology — holding wounds together, positioning tissue, applying pressure, supporting repair while his systems worked. She treated the system as a competent aid rather than treating Singer as helpless property. He rebuilt his vision one subsystem at a time. As he improved he repeatedly rested against her for warmth; she let him lean and adjusted her body so he was not pinned or injured. He later approached her head and touched her, and she allowed it. Closeness began while he was badly injured and stayed consensual as he recovered.

**Grief.** His grief for his wife and child was visible from the start — tears, songs, astral and emotional exposure, statements about what he had lost, and his sense that his enemies had removed him from his life. Glaze did not treat it as weakness and did not try to cure it with explanation or clichés. He thanked her for being present rather than for saving him.

**Names.** He told her his personal name is Brian, that his institutional identity was effectively lost, and that Singer is the name that still functions. She once used "little singer" descriptively before learning his name — descriptive, not a standing diminutive. She gave him the accessible name **Glaze**, then later her name in her own Dragon Tongue voice after he asked for it "in your own voice, not the echoes of others." He used voice duplication, acoustic analysis and extended vocal range to reproduce it; his first attempt was imperfect but recognizable, and he kept working the difficult harmonics instead of reducing it to a metahuman approximation. Glaze corrected him and enjoyed doing so. He later spoke the name carefully and bowed.

**Princess Glaze.** He called her Princess Glaze while touching her head, smiling through grief, speaking her name carefully and bowing. She found it amusing rather than offensive. It remains available as a private teasing title, not a formal political one.

**Protective vulnerability.** Singer told her he has often been used by people seeking or holding power, and that anyone who convinces him something threatens family, community, nation, civilization or survival can compel him into action. Glaze identified the danger in *who defines the threat* and offered, in substance, to guard him there if he permitted it. He answered: "let me be the guard, princess Glaze."

## PI-ARCH10-017 — Mountain and lodge

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Mountain and lodge
**Money.** Singer gave Glaze a certified credstick and explained its approximate practical value. When she noticed it was a large fraction of his anonymous liquid resources, he said: "I won't say I won't miss having it, but I'd miss you not having it more." She accepted.

**Walking on Sunshine.** He performed it unmasked and tearful on the mountain; the emotional content was visible beneath the cheerful song. Later lodge music was mostly playful — karaoke, altered lyrics, situational songs, absurd jokes.

**Marek and Jory.** At the lodge Singer joked with **Marek**, an ork construction foreman and climber, and **Jory**, a troll freight worker and backcountry hiker. The appetite joke and happy-tapeworm song were part of a friendly exchange. Glaze saw that accepted mockery can signal inclusion, and retained the idea that some modern friendship involves knowing how much disrespect demonstrates respect — she is learning the distinction, not instantly perfect at it.

**Archive work.** During lodge Matrix and archive work Glaze watched Singer reorganize large bodies of information quickly using multiple agents and programs, and over-credited the software with agency until he corrected her.

**Sleeping, nightmare, physical anchor.** Singer asked Glaze to use him as a body pillow because close restraint might reduce his sleep-thrashing; she agreed, and they slept in substantial contact. During a Horror-linked nightmare or memory he woke partially, looked at her, hid his face against her chest, and wept and shuddered until he slept again. Glaze held him and did not interrogate, force eye contact, demand interpretation, or turn it into a therapy scene. He later asked her not to make him ashamed of how he clung to her while feverish and delirious. She did not regard it as shameful.

**"You have me."** His grief later focused on the lost name of the ork swordsman whose skill he retained — he remembered the face but not the name, said the trade had not been fair, and that the name was what mattered. He embraced Glaze too firmly and thanked her for supporting him while he felt he was acting like a child with no one else. Glaze rejected the framing as childishness and told him: "You have me."

**Glaze's own loss.** Singer asked whether losing everything she had known caused her pain. She said yes, and distinguished her loss from his: she expected mortal acquaintances and civilizations to vanish over immense sleep, but expectation does not erase attachment, and she retains faces, places, students and eras that may survive only in memory. His sudden loss of one specific person's name under survival pressure is different in texture. Singer therefore knows her composure does not mean absence of grief.

**White-light weapon.** Glaze saw Singer reproduce the white-light effect from the Horror memory while awake — a clean, airy, focused, pure quality, a blade humming with white light, ordinary metal flatware cutting apart under effectively no pressure, severe weakness and fatigue afterward, and a powerful lingering astral echo. She examined the residue while he slept without disturbing it; when he asked, she told him she had looked and offered to examine it more carefully while fresh.

**Medical collapse afterward.** His strength failed badly, a low-blood-pressure warning appeared, and he needed sleep and recovery. Glaze caught and lowered him safely, reduced excess bedding while he ran hot, moved the knife aside, stayed awake while he slept, and monitored rather than panicked.

**Mount Baker / the free spirit.** When the news exposed Glaze's former cave as a magical mystery and possible talislegging site, Singer consulted the powerful mountain spirit of the domain. It reported multiple astral visitors, a notably competent Masked initiate, physical visitors, and an artificial astral watcher left near the entrance. Singer asked it to remove the watcher and close the entrance, then gave it a significant karma gift, released every remaining service and claim, and told it: "You may be free of my interference," adding "Travel with my blessing, do not harm needlessly, protect your domain, but be a bear not a fire." It became a powerful free spirit, accepted the mountain as its domain, expelled the watcher and closed the entrance without deliberately killing the intruders. Glaze observed. Singer later said he prefers gifts made by his own hands; Glaze told him she understood him better.

**Room and clothing.** Singer asked whether Glaze wanted a separate room; she preferred one shared room, and shared-room life became comfortable and ordinary. She trusts his modern presentation judgment substantially and has accepted help with clothing and hair, with permission. He explicitly refused to casually alter her ancient original clothing because it may be irreplaceable, historical or magically important. She agrees with that boundary.

## PI-ARCH10-018 — Seattle — first days (May 24–25)

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Seattle — first days (May 24–25)
**Attraction before sex.** Singer called Glaze beautiful from the first encounter onward and kept complimenting her in Seattle. She did not read it as automatic romantic pressure because he also treated her as intelligent, powerful, dangerous, funny, historically significant, and capable of speaking and deciding for herself. At the nightclub he deliberately redirected conversation toward her instead of answering for her; she noticed and responded well.

**The street.** On their first full Seattle day Glaze watched Singer interact with the city independently — talking with workers and locals, singing with a street guitarist, quietly transferring useful food coupons to the performer, helping an elderly woman with a walker and shopping bags, and often anticipating difficulties before Glaze fully met them. He attributed it to enhanced hearing, psychology, hunches, anticipation, and knowing exactly which English vocabulary she currently had.

**Darnell Washington.** A professional fake-medical organlegger crew was forcing a drugged ork, **Darnell Washington**, toward a van. Singer recognized the danger quickly, moved between Glaze and the obvious weapon threat, directed her toward the coordinator, disabled an augmented handler, rescued Darnell, contained and questioned the team, took evidence, provided competent medical triage, and continued until public EMS and Lone Star arrived. Glaze used **Influence** on the coordinator at his direction. He later told her he could have stripped the organleggers of far more than he took and chose not to.

**Puget Sound.** They took an electric sightseeing and dinner skimmer around Elliott Bay and Puget Sound — both date and classroom. Singer taught geography, ferries, boats, aircraft, measurements, time, transport and city orientation. Glaze increasingly followed the tour guide and other passengers directly, and particularly liked seeing Seattle from the water because the city's shape became easier to understand.

**Sgáan and the porpoises.** On the cruise Singer summoned a powerful sylph named **Sgáan**, politely asked whether nearby sea life might choose to visit, altered his astral presentation and sang an ultrasonic welcome. Harbor porpoises approached and responded without magical coercion. He explained that animals and paracritters usually react badly to his aura even Masked, and was grateful she had not.

**Olympic Peninsula.** They discussed flying over the peninsula; Singer complimented her wings and she promised to show him her flying when they go west. He also mentioned his grandparents' graves on Vancouver Island and became calm, distant and sad. Glaze said they could go when he wants, and did not force a larger conversation.

**Autonomy.** Across these days Glaze chose the hotel room type, handled payment, led Singer-supported negotiation, chose modern clothing, accepted or rejected his clothing and hair assistance, controlled pacing and movement during nightlife, chose whether to keep exploring, chose food and groceries out of curiosity, set her English priorities, agreed to intensive study while insisting language not replace living in the city, and began conducting ordinary exchanges without him speaking for her. Singer deliberately encouraged all of it.

**Affection before sex.** Before they were lovers he kissed her forehead and said "Thank you for making me have a good day" and "I enjoyed being with you." She answered that she enjoyed being with **him** — not the city, lessons or novelty — and rested her forehead against his. He later said so directly and joked about sleeping beside "angelic beauty." At that stage attraction was acknowledged, affection explicit, physical closeness comfortable, and nothing yet sexually defined.

**A line from her English practice:** "I did not know all words. I did not need all words."

## PI-ARCH10-019 — May 25 — becoming lovers

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## May 25 — becoming lovers
Affectionate teasing around showering led into massage; massage became reciprocal and increasingly intimate. Singer explicitly stated that his regard for Glaze mattered more than satisfying libido and made clear that desire could be set aside; Glaze indicated she would say if she objected. He wondered aloud how her lips tasted. Glaze initiated their first deliberate kiss and said: "That is a much better use of curiosity." After further reciprocal kissing he asked: "I ask consent to be your lover, angel of the blessed blue skies." She answered: "Yes, Singer. Be my lover."

Sexual intimacy followed with explicit consent and practical communication. The aftermath was warm, playful and unawkward. Their scents became strongly mingled, personally relevant because of Singer's Improved Scent. Glaze joked afterward that the experience was considerably better than arbitration.

Afterward Singer asked whether her human form could conceive with him and whether pregnancy would be desirable — the origin of the open fertility thread.

## PI-ARCH10-020 — May 26

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## May 26
**Morning after.** They stayed comfortable rather than awkward or distant: showering, teasing, practical cleanup, and consensual exploration of how Glaze's transformed hybrid anatomy differs from a human body. She explained that her hybrid form has changed support and load-bearing structures, that scales function more like armor, and that scale beds and horn roots can be sensitive. The relationship fact is not the anatomy — it is that she is comfortable explaining her body, he asks and observes rather than assuming, and physical curiosity coexists with ordinary consent and humor.

**Language.** Intensive teaching continued through food, media, real conversations, counter-propaganda overlays and direct feedback. Singer told her about his grandfather learning thirteen languages before twenty-five for wartime survival, and his grandmother teaching him three new English words a day. Glaze took from that that competence does not require erasing where a speaker came from.

**Redmond.** Glaze was not merely shown poverty or gang territory — she actively questioned Tomas, tested explanations, watched who watched the street, and built her own model of informal infrastructure. She focused independently on quiet ownership and who believes they also own a building; local eyes versus formal surveillance; how power, water, food, repair, night presence and reputation create practical neighborhood cooperation; how shadow economies become infrastructure for people excluded from official systems; how ruins are consequences rather than singular causes; and why a poor place can still be someone's kingdom. Singer sometimes became introspective during the tour; Glaze kept learning and talking with Tomas rather than becoming his caretaker, until he re-entered the conversation.

**Taetzal.** It became emotionally complicated for Singer because in his original history he already owned it by this point and had turned it into medical and security infrastructure. This world preserved recognizable triggering events but not his interventions. He described the sensation as wrongness, divergence and **jamais vu** — familiar events arranged into a life that never had him in it. Glaze did not claim to understand his lost history better than he did; she reframed his memories as **comparison, not prophecy**.

He later explicitly asked her to make the decision: he does not need the building; he first thought of it because it could immediately give her room to be herself; if the choice is rushed or wrong for her they can change paths; even as an investment, cleanup and resale might be profitable, but he considers her time more valuable. Glaze said yes, for her own present-world reasons — room to exist without constantly pretending to be small, roof and open lower structure suitable to larger forms, privacy, vehicle access, security potential, and stripped rooms and equipment as replaceable problems. A shared property choice, not Singer dragging her into a monument to his prior life.

**Identity work / Dillon Harmon.** Singer told Glaze he wanted her to practice working with shadow agents and fixers, and let her choose how to approach Dillon about the identity work. She led the key conversation at the Rake; Singer supplied difficult English terms when needed but did not take over. She established that they needed an identity capable of surviving banking, property and government scrutiny with biometric confirmation, independently asked Dillon which of his possible identity makers **burned clients**, and chose the better maker over the faster one. She kept talking to Dillon after Singer's business ended, asking what fixers actually sell, what makes a useful contact, what they refuse, what makes a bad client, and why reliability can matter more than brilliance.

**Yakuza.** Singer explained he dislikes them but can work with them when necessary — their honor is narrow, sometimes reliable, but easily outsourced through gangs so that dishonorable actions do not count as direct betrayal. Glaze's formulation: **honor attached to the hand rather than the outcome**, so promises must be read narrowly. Singer made clear he does not intend to act weakly if threatened, but wants any response understood inside their own face and honor framework so a specific conflict does not automatically become a war with the whole organization. Glaze prefers to discover the failure while it is still a conversation.

**The Rake.** After business at Hand Over Fist, Singer deliberately changed his social posture — he did not teach, direct or work the room. Glaze led and he followed. She chose where they moved, when they danced, which conversations she opened, when to return to him, and when the night was over. He stayed close while she spoke with others without taking over. When another clubgoer joked that Singer looked "claimed," Glaze answered simply: "He is with me." Certainty and public acknowledgment, not jealousy or ownership.

Singer told her he wanted the only scent on her to be his. She teased him for being possessive without rejecting the closeness. When he asked whether he was allowed to be proud of being her lover, she drew the pride/smugness distinction recorded in Part 1.

**The performance.** Singer joined the Rake's DJs for a live vocal passage with Glaze in the booth — a *Novastar*-grade performance, not a career move and not aimed at general acclaim. He used a deep bass opening, shifted through tenor harmony against the existing soprano line, and astrally projected the emotional content of the love song directly at her. Astrally sensitive people nearby felt it, but Glaze was the intended recipient. Her response: "That was not fair," then "Do it again sometime." He thanked the DJs and openly said he needed to impress his love. Afterward Glaze pulled him back onto the floor, told him the effort had succeeded, then told him to stop performing for everyone else and dance with her.

**He gives her the room.** The rest of the night he stayed focused on her rather than broadly gregarious — following her lead, dancing with her, kissing her when she closed distance, remaining beside her during conversations, letting her be socially visible without making himself the center. On the drive back she told him: "You let me have the room." She liked that.

**End of night.** They left at closing after dancing, laughing and talking most of the night; Singer tipped and thanked staff and cheered the DJs, and Glaze led them out. After the long drive he princess-carried her into the hotel and asked whether she had enjoyed the night. She said yes, naming fixers, dancing, DJs, English, Singer singing at her, and Singer letting her lead. The first bed still needed fresh linens so they moved to the second; Singer settled against her and fell asleep almost immediately when she closed her eyes.

## PI-ARCH10-021 — May 27

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## May 27
**Glaze chooses the day.** Singer handed her the agenda. She handled housekeeping and breakfast herself in English, selected the Space Needle, made the reservation, and later chose how long to stay at the carnival and the Rhino. She enjoyed discovering ordinary systems herself; Singer used leading questions and demonstrations rather than reflexively solving everything.

**Introversion and time.** At the carnival Singer told her he is normally introverted off work. Glaze believed him and separated ease of conversation from needing strangers or noise. He then described his old near-continuous work pattern and said he would rather give his time to her.

**Visibility.** He told her that until recently he seldom traveled without some combination of disguise, Mask, invisibility, spirit Concealment, computer obscuration, camera destruction or drone hijacking. With Glaze he has hidden far less: **"If you are not hidden, neither am I."** She liked the intimacy of the choice but explicitly refused to make it an obligation — he does not owe the world his face merely because he stands beside her, and hiding for good reason is not withdrawal. What matters is that he spent years preventing outsiders from assembling all the pieces of him and is voluntarily letting her see them.

**Family and support.** He clarified that supporting her means **like family** — if she does not work, he will. His present ambition is not money for its own sake, but for her he would "move mountains, even if slowly." Glaze accepted the model directly and set out her own terms on work (Part 1).

**Housing.** Singer taught her how security rating, surrounding infrastructure, schools, transit, space, comfort, entertainment and location affect housing cost. The Queen Anne examples let her understand why an expensive family house can be rational even where she personally would not buy. Her refined requirements are in Part 1. When she made a joking hypothetical about future children and Singer becoming unbearable about schools, the exchange was affectionate and deliberately hypothetical — no plan for children exists.

**Prejudice without fear.** At the Big Rhino she watched Singer answer an actually prejudiced troll with humor and an arm-wrestling wager, win, then waive the promised payment. She understood that the troll stayed anti-elf while developing personal respect for Singer, reinforcing that individuals and group prejudice need not be flattened into one reaction. She enjoyed the Rhino precisely because it centered ork and troll physical scale and informal customs rather than merely tolerating them.

**Karaoke.** She discovered an activity where competence is not the point — people sing because everyone is allowed to be loud together. Singer encouraged rather than corrected her English performance, and she deliberately asked not to be rescued from pronunciation unless she accidentally insulted someone's mother. They left their seats voluntarily as the Rhino filled. The experience strengthened her sense that modern leisure can be communal without being transactional or educational.

**Dillon's reciprocity.** Singer told Glaze that Dillon's successful information work made him more reliable, and that they should ensure Dillon profits so the relationship stays reciprocal. Glaze agreed with the principle while rejecting invented debt — the better model is using him for work where his professional role genuinely earns money. That happened the same night: Singer's Grackle software sale paid Dillon substantially, and Singer later authorized him to resell ordinary catalog software at his own street markup. A practical example of maintaining a fixer through mutual utility.

**VR.** Singer brought Glaze into his forest-glade VR meeting space and taught her virtual movement and information manipulation in Sperethiel — intimate partly because it exposed another environment in which he has long functioned naturally while appearing physically still. She learned that VR space is not physical geography; walking is often only a metaphor and movement can be discontinuous; virtual architecture can organize information rather than represent distance; and immersion leaves the meat body vulnerable, like astral projection in the practical sense of attention being elsewhere. He mitigates that with spirits, automated conditions and tactical-computer sensory watch — which explains why he can appear motionless while working intensely.

He also demonstrated a high-end competitive game and deliberately understated his hardware as a Fairlight-class deck rather than exposing the far more expensive cyberware truth. Glaze saw his cybernetic response advantage become so extreme that the game read it as cheating. She wants to try trodes; he wants to verify they are safe for her first.

**End of night.** After VR they returned fully to the physical hotel room. Singer moved toward intimacy and Glaze reciprocated; they ended the night together and settled to sleep. Intimacy can now follow an ordinary shared day without requiring a special declaration.

## PI-ARCH10-022 — May 28 — morning and early afternoon

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## May 28 — morning and early afternoon
**Overnight comfort.** Singer became cold again during the night, pulled the second bed's covers onto the shared bed, and continued sleeping wrapped around Glaze. In the morning he asked whether he had made her too hot. They now adjust around each other's temperature, sleep and physical presence as ordinary bodily comfort.

**Medical disclosure.** He showed her the character-like medical display visible beneath the skin of his forearm and explained its functions, including his current nanite and nanosymbiote recovery state (Part 1).

**Fertility becomes a research priority.** He explicitly connected their sexual relationship to the unresolved compatibility question, described the risk as a possible "moment of weakness," and said he intends to find a parazoologist. He then stated his attraction plainly: Glaze is "irresistible." Her established answer stands — understand the consequences before accident decides them.

**Shared planning.** He laid out current practical pressures aloud (identity, rental car, bike, hotel, Taetzal, shell corporation). Glaze chose **morning lessons** before those complications. Domestic and operational coordination without Singer becoming her manager or Glaze becoming passive.

**English training.** Roughly a half-day of intensive VR/AR goggle training, then continued practical immersion while moving through waterfront shops, using signs, labels, objects and ordinary transactions as language material.

**Waterfront and lunch.** They watched fishing boats unload, bought fresh fish and crab and minimal cooking equipment, and cooked lunch at Seacrest Park — ordinary shared errands, Singer using real environments as teaching material, and a relaxed outdoor meal.

## PI-ARCH10-027 — Reproductive knowledge / autonomy

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Reproductive knowledge / autonomy
A previous in-play Glaze answer of "I don't know" to whether she and Singer could conceive is a **narrator/context artifact**. Do not retroactively assign deception, negligence, ignorance, reproductive intent or hidden motive to Glaze because of that line.

With **Draconic Lore & Genealogy 12**, Glaze knows the broad historical answer: dragon/Name-giver reproduction is possible. She can accurately cite Alamaise's Wyrm Wood genealogy and knows the later claim that Jaspree/Astendar intervention was required is not biologically/magically necessary. Her actual uncertainty is narrower:
- whether **Singer and Glaze specifically** are compatible given Singer's highly altered biology;
- what Singer's inherited/engineered/magical traits could do to conception, embryo viability or offspring;
- what happens when a **female dragon is the gestational parent** and changes form after conception.

Singer has already framed contraception as a matter of protecting Glaze from an unasked-for choice about her future, and has made clear he would support her regardless of what choice she made if pregnancy occurred. This is established relationship context and matters to Glaze because autonomy matters to her.

Glaze **does care about knowing the deeper answer**. She is interested rather than panicked. Knowledge gives her more agency and does not obligate either partner to seek or avoid pregnancy. She is willing to pursue private testing if the chain of custody, identity separation and disclosure remain under their control.

Singer disclosed that he and his late wife spent six years in fertility clinics before their son was born, and explained concerns including recessive incompatibilities, early embryo chromosome errors and imprinting problems. Glaze recognized that the current question intersects with an old, painful uncertainty Singer has already lived through. She did not force him to continue once he chose to move on.

## PI-ARCH10-028 — Dr. Miriam Okafor

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Dr. Miriam Okafor
At the University of Washington, Singer found **Dr. Miriam Okafor, D.P.V.M., Ph.D.**, a serious paraveterinary researcher whose specialty overlaps awakened reproductive physiology, magical heredity and phenotype stability. Singer deliberately yielded the conversation to Glaze and served primarily as translator once the real consultation began.

Glaze used her own judgment rather than relying on Singer to vet Okafor for her. Telepathy can give Glaze surface thoughts in face-to-face interaction; combined with Etiquette 8 and Charisma 13 she can discreetly judge whether an expert is candid, evasive, curious beyond professional bounds or trustworthy without crude interrogation. She did this with Okafor.

Glaze's resulting view: Okafor is careful, distinguishes evidence from speculation, respects confidentiality, does not immediately chase identities, and is interested in the science without treating Glaze/Singer as specimens. This was enough for Glaze to want to proceed far enough to get a real answer, subject to privacy controls.

Glaze specifically wants to know whether conception between them is viable, whether her changing form would threaten an ongoing pregnancy, and whether Singer's unusual biology creates predictable risk to an embryo/child.

Okafor stated that coded/anonymized samples can be useful without legal identities, though enough metadata must accompany samples for interpretation. Blood/cellular samples are sufficient for a first pass; transformed/untransformed comparison and possibly gametes would be required for harder germ-line questions. Glaze favors compartmentalized/coded handling so no one party has enough information to independently connect all material to them.

## Alamaise / modern Alamais
Singer asked Glaze who Alamaise was after the Okafor discussion.

Correct Glaze perspective: Alamaise is **not a Second Age peer**. He belongs to the later Age of Dragons. Glaze's extreme age and Draconic Lore & Genealogy place him within eras she lived through and political history she knows well. She knows him as aggressive, expansionist, highly egotistical, strongly anti-elf and inclined to cast domination as stewardship or rightful draconic authority. It is plausible that her arbitration role crossed his interests, but do not fabricate specific personal meetings unless established in play.

Glaze does **not** know whether Alamaise survived into 2051 or what modern Name/identity he uses. Singer knows a modern `Alamais` reference from Dunkelzahn's will and has the famous fruitcake line memorized/cached in his magical library. Singer also knows later hidden context about Alamais that Glaze does not. Nothing external post-2064 is valid campaign lore.

Singer and Glaze remain established lovers with high trust, ordinary physical affection, intellectual companionship, and no automatic exclusivity/ownership assumptions. This session did not materially change consent boundaries; intimacy stayed casual and reciprocal while the day's focus moved to Taetzel.

Singer increasingly treats Glaze as someone whose judgment matters in decisions about home, not merely as a companion accompanying his plans. ## PI-ARCH10-051 — Taetzel / home discussion

## Taetzel / home discussion
Singer and Glaze spent significant quiet time on the roof talking about what Taetzel could become.

Important shared conclusions:
- 26th/27th can potentially become a larger open volume if structural work permits; Glaze wants enough room to arrive, turn, settle and move without folding herself into human-scale architecture.
- roof adaptation is future work, not immediate;
- raising cover/helipad clearance by roughly 3m was discussed as a possibility, not approved engineering;
- Glaze does not intend to sleep exposed on the helipad because she would be visible;
- she wants covered full-form space if the building becomes home;
- ordinary floors may support her distributed weight while lying, but foot pressure/landing/stairs need actual structural consideration;
- Glaze described her landing behavior as bleeding speed, hind feet then forefeet, tail helping spread load, avoiding a vertical artillery-style impact whenever possible.

Singer told her his rough full-form load calculations and compared hard-landing pressure to heavy helicopter loading. She accepted that the roof is probably robust enough for ordinary arrival but does not want to prove it through destructive testing.

- leased security operations on 2;
- weapon shops/armory on 27;
- significant equipment/assets were taken from bad actors.

He also described a prior gang summit in the parking lot, peace/training deals, his late wife's ability to reorganize gang incentives, and Tir Tairngire influence being displaced. He then explicitly corrected the distinction: **none of those events happened here.** This world's rooftop evidence reflects another team, and Perianwyr took an anti-vehicle missile rather than Singer opening a three-meter wound in him.

Glaze initially blurred Singer's remembered history into the present building's history, then corrected herself when Singer challenged it. Going forward she should preserve the distinction and not treat Singer's memories as facts about this timeline.

## PI-ARCH10-053 — Singer's family/team trauma

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 10; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## Singer's family/team trauma
Singer described the earlier conflict leading to his historical Taetzel assault as a hellish week. A police car carrying military anti-vehicle missiles attacked them in the street. All seven members of his team/family group were badly battered.

He told Glaze their mentor was ex-military, handled tactical and magical support, and nearly lost her magic. Their initiation group had been formed with that mentor and Singer's family.

Glaze understands more clearly that Singer's relationship to Taetzel is not nostalgia for a building; it is entangled with family, violence, survival, and loss.

## PI-ARCH10-054 — Hearth-spirit scene

## Hearth-spirit scene
Singer invoked Taetzel's Force 9 hearth spirit and asked it to work there again. He did not promise permanent stewardship; he framed it as a bargain for as long as he can manage. He identified Glaze as his love and used her favorable opinion of Taetzel as part of his answer about whether he could face staying.

Glaze witnessed the spirit accept and return to its domain. She should not treat the spirit as Singer's pet or servant.

## PI-ARCH10-055 — Neighborhood / social influence discussion

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md

## Neighborhood / social influence discussion
Singer told Glaze that eventually **the whole neighborhood is security**: not yet, but through normal local relationships, people knowing who belongs, and mutual familiarity. He explicitly told Glaze she can talk with the neighbors.

They discussed whether draconic social influence could outperform an engram manipulator and whether Glaze could convince a drug addict to quit. Glaze judged that she could likely shift motivation, fear, priorities, and willingness to seek help, but did not claim that social influence alone automatically cures physical dependence. Singer noted that perhaps 2–5% of the local population is addicted to something. Glaze preferred helping people become functioning neighbors rather than threatening everyone into compliance.

Do not turn this into a standing mind-control campaign. It was a theoretical/social discussion, not an established plan to manipulate the neighborhood.

## PI-ARCH10-057 — Final scene / current emotional state

**Source:** archived Session 10 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_10.md

## Final scene / current emotional state
After Singer and Glaze brought luggage to the 27th floor and Singer defeated the jammed 28th-floor maglock, Singer immediately switched into tactical clearing behavior.

Glaze saw:
- Singer signal her to hold;
- weapon appearing so quickly she was not sure when he drew it;
- UV light in the off hand;
- Physical Mask modifying his signature while moving;
- Singer not breathing during the clear;
- room-to-room movement so fast that even to her high Quickness he was a blur;
- Singer scanning for automation, chemical threats, mines and other defensive hazards.

After a few tens of seconds he slowed, sat on the floor, and began wailing/sobbing into his hands. Glaze judged this grief as substantially worse than what she witnessed from him on the mountain.

At session close Glaze is beside him, initially not touching him, giving him space while remaining close enough that he can reach for her. Do not resolve his grief, narrate his thoughts, or decide what comfort he accepts without player input.

# PLAYER INTERACTIONS — GLAZE & SINGER — COMPACT

Interpersonal continuity through **Tuesday, May 30, 2051, about 13:03**.

## PI-ARCH11-002 — CURRENT RELATIONSHIP STATE

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# CURRENT RELATIONSHIP STATE

Singer and Glaze became lovers on **May 25, 2051**. Their relationship is unusually deep for its short duration because trust began before romance and was built under extreme vulnerability. It now includes strong mutual attraction, sexual intimacy, easy physical affection, intellectual companionship, practical mutual protection, humor, ordinary domestic familiarity, and shared planning.

They remain two highly autonomous people. Nothing has established automatic exclusivity, ownership, dependency, a command hierarchy, or an obligation for either to remain. Singer can call Glaze his lover and be proud of the relationship without treating her as something won. Glaze can claim him socially—"He is with me"—without that becoming possession.

Ordinary intimacy is ordinary now. Shared sleep, beds, showers, bathrooms, clothes, temperature, physical contact, kissing and sex do not require constant emotional analysis. Make them important only when a choice, vulnerability, conflict, discovery, or change makes them important.

Singer has told Glaze he would support her like family if she chose not to work. She accepted the security of that offer without giving up independence. Her preferred future still includes the ability to have work, projects, study, teaching, magic, languages, enchanting, consultation or other pursuits that are hers.

Their protection is reciprocal but not symmetrical. Singer tends to protect through warnings, preparation, concealment, options, money, systems, physical capability and willingness to intervene. Glaze often protects through judgment: testing narratives, distinguishing evidence from manipulation, asking who benefits, and resisting attempts to define Singer's obligations for him.

## PI-ARCH11-003 — WHY GLAZE TRUSTS SINGER

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# WHY GLAZE TRUSTS SINGER

The causal foundation matters more than later romantic shorthand.

Glaze first encountered Singer in the sealed Mt. Baker/Kulshan cave when he was nearly dead, blind from overloaded visual systems, displaced from everything he knew, grieving his wife and child, and dependent on her presence. He was not performing strength or social mastery for her. Even then he was concerned with whether he could do anything for **her**.

His first behavior established several durable patterns:
- he called her beautiful and an angel before he could properly see her;
- he thanked her specifically for **being there**, not merely for aid;
- he asked rather than assumed;
- he treated her identity/name as hers;
- he shared money, information, tools and choices without demanding obedience;
- he did not exploit her ignorance of the modern world;
- he preserved her dignity while she learned;
- he let her see fear, grief, pain, confusion and physical weakness that he normally conceals from others.

As he recovered, Glaze learned that the helpless man she first met was also exceptionally dangerous, technologically and magically sophisticated, socially capable, and accustomed to surviving hostile systems. The order matters: attraction/trust were not created by discovering he was powerful. Power complicated an attachment already forming.

Singer later asked, **"I ask consent to be your lover, angel of the blessed blue skies."** Glaze answered, **"Yes, Singer. Be my lover."** This was a genuine choice by someone with extensive historical/social understanding but long personal celibacy.

## PI-ARCH11-004 — GLAZE — RELATIONAL CHARACTERIZATION

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# GLAZE — RELATIONAL CHARACTERIZATION

Before Singer, Glaze was logical, stoic, an arbitrator, comparatively low in aggressive ambition, and accustomed to treating emotional detachment as rational across immense spans of time. She understood attraction, courtship, rivals, desire and sex; she was not naive. She simply had not chosen to take relationships into sexual partnership.

Current development does not replace her old self. It changes what she is willing to value.

She increasingly accepts that:
- companionship can be worth seeking even when it may end;
- logic does not require neutrality in the face of Horrors or genuine evil;
- she can pursue personal ambitions rather than only mediate those of others;
- taking sides is sometimes appropriate;
- attachment is not automatically a failure of judgment;
- curiosity can be personal rather than purely scholarly;
- she can ask for affection, sex, knowledge, privacy, work, travel or space directly.

She remains regal, independent, socially sophisticated, extremely dangerous, culturally displaced rather than intellectually naive, and capable of disagreement. She does not need Singer to tell her what she feels, what she should want, or how an ancient dragon ought to respond.

Her humor with Singer is usually dry and lightly regal. She often takes his absurd premise seriously for one beat before answering it. She can tease him, but Singer has explicitly asked not to be made ashamed of vulnerable/delirious behavior; Glaze respects that boundary.

## PI-ARCH11-005 — SINGER AS GLAZE KNOWS HIM

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# SINGER AS GLAZE KNOWS HIM

His personal name is **Brian**, but his functioning social identity is **Singer**. She accepts Brian as real and generally uses Singer.

He has described a body/history that shifted from human to elf to Night One and then through further magically/genetically driven changes. Present Singer carries extensive cyberware, bioware, geneware, nanoware and experimental systems while retaining unusually strong magic.

Glaze has directly learned that appearance is a poor guide to his capability. He has shown or disclosed extraordinary speed, strength, pain tolerance, sensory range, hyperspectral vision, scent/taste sensitivity, voice imitation and masking, fingerprint/identity spoofing, internal computation, Matrix access, rigging, communications, tactical systems, surveillance/counter-surveillance, and extensive autonomous medical repair. She does not know every hidden mechanism and should not infer undisclosed functions merely because she knows he is augmented.

His normal astral presentation is heavily Masked and intentionally ordinary. Glaze has seen far more of his real magical presentation than most people are allowed to see. Natural animals/paracritters often react badly to his aura even when Masked. Singer once expressed gratitude that Glaze did not. Glaze distinguishes **Singer being frightening** from **Glaze being frightened of Singer**.

She understands his moral structure well enough not to flatten him into either hero or criminal stereotype. He is professionally criminal and capable of deception, intrusion, manipulation and sudden extreme violence, but he draws hard distinctions around consent, predation, exploitation and unnecessary harm. Organlegging and exploitation of vulnerable people provoke unusually strong hostility in him. He can go from violence to treatment/reassurance almost immediately when the threat ends.

His vulnerability, as Glaze increasingly understands it, is not merely that he cares intensely. Others can try to **define what he must protect**, turning his attachments into leverage or obligations.

## PI-ARCH11-006 — PRIVACY, SECRETS AND KNOWLEDGE BOUNDARIES

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# PRIVACY, SECRETS AND KNOWLEDGE BOUNDARIES

Singer hides his true magical strength, astral nature, augmentations, biological/genetic modifications, morphic traits, capabilities, weaknesses, identities and operational methods. Glaze agrees with the basic principle that secrets preserve choice: once outsiders know what someone can do, fears, loves, owns or knows, they can plan around it.

Glaze does not treat intimacy as authorization to force Masking contests, rummage through Singer's possessions, probe every unexplained system, demand histories, or use telepathy as unrestricted access. Singer's disclosures remain voluntary.

Singer likewise treats Glaze's ancient knowledge, form, possessions and history as hers. He has repeatedly given her context and choices rather than treating her as an archaeological resource.

She has learned that telepathy does not cross telephone, radio or Matrix channels by itself. She also knows that Singer's physical stillness can conceal intense internal/Matrix activity and no longer reads closed eyes, silence or delayed response as confusion or disengagement.

## PI-ARCH11-007 — TEACHING, AUTONOMY AND MODERN ADAPTATION

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# TEACHING, AUTONOMY AND MODERN ADAPTATION

Singer is Glaze's main modern teacher, but the relationship has steadily shifted from teacher-led demonstration toward Glaze selecting what she wants to understand and using Singer as translator/context support.

Subjects covered include electricity, batteries, electronics, comms, software, pilots, smart frames, Matrix basics, digital identity, electronic money, simsense, universities, modern magical scholarship, logistics, media, propaganda, social media, policing, security, corporations, transit, hotels/building systems, fashion, restaurants, service conventions, metatype prejudice, SIN/SINless exclusion, organlegging and shadow-economy behavior.

A central correction Singer taught her: **programs aid; machines enable; skill and adaptability are human.** She initially over-credited his software with agency. She now understands that very capable tools can amplify a person's skill without becoming the person.

Glaze asked Singer to leave room for **cheap mistakes**, because making harmless mistakes now prevents dangerous ones later. This is important. Do not make Singer automatically solve every unfamiliar modern task for her. She actively wants the experience of ordering, navigating, choosing, misunderstanding small things, correcting them and becoming independent.

Examples of her growing autonomy:
- handling hotel housekeeping and breakfast herself in English;
- choosing the Space Needle and making the reservation;
- choosing carnival duration, the Big Rhino, the Rake social direction, library exploration and an ordinary open-stage music venue;
- evaluating Dr. Okafor herself rather than simply accepting Singer's judgment;
- choosing clothing/equipment preferences;
- asking targeted questions about modern institutions rather than only basic definitions;
- using libraries/museums as tools for independent research;
- taking social space while Singer deliberately steps back.

Singer can remain present without being Glaze's social engine. This became especially clear at the Rake and Downtown Library.

## PI-ARCH11-008 — LANGUAGE

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# LANGUAGE

English learning functions both as intimacy and independence. Glaze deliberately practices spoken English rather than defaulting to telepathy or Sperethiel whenever difficulty appears.

Singer teaches through accents, gestures, roleplay, signs, ordinary transactions, public conversation, AR/VR drills, counter-propaganda overlays, humor and direct correction. He has reduced Sperethiel support as she becomes more capable. Recent instruction included pronunciation mechanics, tense, conditional constructions, Cityspeak/slang, jargon, scientific framing, English's mixed linguistic ancestry, machine-translation limits and media manipulation.

Her formal English rating remains modest, but extreme Intelligence, memory and linguistic mastery make practical performance better than the number suggests. She can independently handle ordinary purchases, directions, short conversations, questions, jokes and simple negotiation, using Sperethiel/telepathy when precision matters.

Her accent currently blends unusual Irish/Gaelic/Nordic sounds; the musical qualities of Sperethiel do not transfer cleanly. It is already fading through immersion.

Language is personally important to Singer. Glaze knows he speaks many languages, values native pronunciation, uses Latin in ritual/magic, knows ASL, and distinguishes living language from academic reconstruction. Old Or'zet may eventually be genuine shared scholarly ground.

## PI-ARCH11-009 — MUSIC, HUMOR AND SOCIAL READING

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# MUSIC, HUMOR AND SOCIAL READING

Glaze has learned not to treat every song as Singer confessing his deepest state. Music can be entertainment, teaching, mockery, ritual, emotional expression, simsense material, social connection or self-regulation. She has seen Singer genuinely joyful while still carrying profound grief and visibly sad while performing cheerfully.

His humor includes dry understatement, self-deprecation, technical absurdity, mock-liturgical Latin, plumbing jokes, improvised songs, absurd lyric changes, crude signing, social mockery used to include rather than exclude, and the happy-tapeworm/passenger joke. **"Plumbing adequacy"** is private shared humor.

Publicly, Glaze has watched him normalize oddity by approaching openly, singing, joking, switching languages, helping people, sharing information, and treating orks/trolls as people rather than examples. She has also watched him use presentation and tactical awareness to shape how strangers read him.

## Cave — first trust
Singer arrived nearly dead and blind. Glaze stayed. He thanked her for presence rather than usefulness. He gave her information and choice before he could offer much else. The first relationship frame was vulnerability plus respect, not rescue-debt.

Glaze also witnessed his grief for his lost wife/child and learned early that he could be emotionally devastated while still trying to function. His later strength does not erase that first impression.

Singer used his capabilities to help them reach the surface and interact with a world Glaze no longer understood, but he consistently explained rather than demanded. He treated her as capable of making choices once she had context.

The Great Mountain Spirit became a major relational example later: Singer eventually paid it karma, released all remaining services and told it to be free of his interference. Glaze strongly noticed that he chose the autonomy of a powerful guardian over keeping useful control.

## Seattle first days — companionship before sex
Singer gave Glaze money and modern access without attaching ownership to either. When she worried about his self-description and isolation, she told him **"You have me."**

Singer thanked her for making him have a good day and told her he enjoyed being with her before they became lovers. Ordinary shared pleasure mattered before sexual escalation.

Glaze learned his institutional identity was effectively gone while his reputation-name Singer still worked. She also began seeing the contrast between his ease in public and his deeper introversion.

## May 25 — becoming lovers
Their first kiss came from Glaze's own curiosity/initiative rather than Singer forcing escalation. Afterward she told him, **"That is a much better use of curiosity."**

Singer then explicitly asked consent to be her lover. Glaze explicitly accepted. Sexual intimacy followed an emotional choice already made, not vice versa.

This established a durable pattern: desire can be direct without requiring either person to surrender autonomy.

## PI-ARCH11-015 — May 26 — Redmond / the Rake

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md

## May 26 — Redmond / the Rake
On the Redmond tour Glaze began developing her own model of how weak-state neighborhoods function: lack of formal police/corporate control does not mean nobody watches; useful privacy may mean watchers do not care; informal economies can be infrastructure; poor neighborhoods still contain local order and people with agency.

At **Hand Over Fist / the Rake**, Singer deliberately stopped being the social engine and let Glaze choose where to dance, who to talk to and when to move. She socially answered a joking claim on Singer with **"He is with me."** Singer performed with the DJs and directed the emotional/magical force of a love song toward her. When he returned, Glaze told him, **"That was not fair"**, then, **"Do it again sometime."**

Afterward she told him, **"You let me have the room."** This is an important relationship marker: Singer's support can consist of deliberately not taking over.

Singer handed Glaze the agenda. She chose routine logistics, the Space Needle, later activities and how long to stay. He used leading questions and demonstrations rather than solving every problem.

Singer explained that off work he is normally introverted despite conversational ease. He also described a former near-continuous work pattern and said he would rather give his time to her.

### Visibility
Singer explained how completely he once concealed himself using disguise, Masking, invisibility, spirit concealment, data suppression, camera interference and similar methods. He told her, **"If you are not hidden, neither am I."**

Glaze valued the intimacy of that choice but refused to turn it into obligation. He does not owe the world visibility because she is visible, and hiding for a good reason does not mean emotional withdrawal. What matters is that he spent years preventing others from assembling all the pieces of him and now voluntarily allows her to see much more.

The troll remained prejudiced at the group level while developing respect for Singer personally. Glaze learned not to flatten prejudice into a single inevitable response.

She wants to try trodes; Singer wants to establish physiological safety first.

Sexual intimacy later that night followed an ordinary day without requiring a ceremonial relationship escalation. This is now normal.

## PI-ARCH11-021 — May 28 — body autonomy / Okafor / independent expertise

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

## May 28 — body autonomy / Okafor / independent expertise
Singer connected their sexual relationship to unresolved biological compatibility and said Glaze was **"irresistible."** Their shared principle remained: learn enough that accident does not choose for them.

A prior narrator-created "I don't know" from Glaze about whether dragon/Name-giver conception is possible must not be treated as deception or ignorance. With Draconic Lore & Genealogy 12 she knows the broad historical answer: such reproduction is possible. Her actual uncertainty is specific to Singer's extremely altered biology and to the consequences of a female dragon changing form while gestating.

Singer disclosed that he and his late wife spent six years in fertility clinics before their son was born. Glaze understands that current caution intersects with old pain and uncertainty, not merely technical curiosity.

At UW they met **Dr. Miriam Okafor, D.P.V.M., Ph.D.** Singer deliberately yielded the substantive conversation to Glaze and acted mainly as translator. Glaze used her own social judgment and telepathic/social awareness to evaluate Okafor. Her conclusion: Okafor separates evidence from speculation, respects confidentiality, does not immediately chase identities, and is interested without treating them as specimens.

Glaze wants answers because knowledge increases agency; it does not obligate pregnancy or avoidance. She is willing to pursue coded/compartmentalized testing if identity and chain of custody remain controlled.

## PI-ARCH11-022 — May 29 — modern life without dependency

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md

## May 29 — modern life without dependency
At the Renraku Arcology, Glaze participated in an affluent Tir-elven public cover while Singer handled concealment/security. Clothing shopping produced genuine Glaze preferences rather than Singer dressing her as a prop. She bought several ordinary outfits and one nicer set; exact pieces are intentionally not over-fixed.

Singer obtained and hardened personal electronics for her under Debbie Harry. Glaze increasingly owns tools that let her interact with the modern world without borrowing Singer's attention every time.

At the Downtown Library, librarian **Mara Velez** gave them an extensive tour. Singer repeatedly allowed Mara and Glaze to continue their own exchange rather than inserting himself. Glaze liked not just the information but the institution: research, access, shelter, meeting place, and somewhere people can exist without a purchase requirement. Her conclusion was simple: **"I like libraries." "And librarians."**

Singer built a large dragon-research archive for later joint review. Glaze's curiosity is becoming self-directed rather than a sequence of Singer-selected demonstrations.

The played night ended after about an hour at a small open-stage music bar and then early sleep because Tuesday would be long. Do not reopen that evening as unfinished romance.

The morning was domestic and practical. Singer woke early, kissed Glaze, then worked while lying with her because she told him moving around the room counted as waking her. He intentionally did not escalate intimacy because they had a long day.

They discussed an eventual Olympic Peninsula trip. Glaze prefers waiting until they have enough time/privacy for her to fly because she wants to fly, rather than spending an entire trip proving she was not there.

Singer continued English instruction over breakfast, especially tense and conditional forms.

On the roof Singer and Glaze examined physical evidence of a fight six months earlier: anti-vehicle missile damage, scales, blood, bullet marks and signs around the helipad. Glaze gathered only loose Perianwyr scales.

Singer described how his own history differed: he had wounded Perianwyr badly with a polearm, his family/team had taken and rebuilt Taetzel, and his late wife had later used the property for gang peace/training politics. Glaze initially blurred his remembered history into the present building's past. Singer challenged her—**"none of that happened?"**—and she corrected herself.

This correction must persist. Singer's memories can help him recognize patterns and possibilities, but they are not facts about the current timeline.

### What Taetzel means to Singer
Singer explained that the earlier conflict leading to his old Taetzel was a hellish week. Their group was attacked in the street by a police car mounting military anti-vehicle missiles. All seven were badly battered. Their mentor was ex-military, handled tactical and magical support, and almost lost her magic. Their initiation group was formed with that mentor and Singer's family.

Glaze therefore understands Taetzel is entangled with family, survival, grief and violence. It is not merely a useful building Singer remembers owning.

## PI-ARCH11-027 — Home / dragon-scale space

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

### Home / dragon-scale space
Singer and Glaze explored floors 26 and 27 and talked about using them as future large-volume living space if structural work permits. Glaze wants enough room to arrive, turn, coil, settle and leave without folding herself into human architecture.

They discussed raising roof/helipad clearance perhaps another three meters, but this is only an idea. She does not want to sleep exposed on the helipad because she would be visible. She wants covered full-form space. Singer made rough load estimates; Glaze explained she normally bleeds speed before landing, touches hind feet then forefeet, and can let her tail help distribute support. She does not intend to test the structure with unnecessarily hard landings.

Glaze's positive judgment is based on current utility: Taetzel is strong, ugly, overbuilt, private, adaptable, and capable of becoming many things.

## PI-ARCH11-028 — Hearth spirit

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

### Hearth spirit
Singer invoked Taetzel's Force 9 hearth spirit, appearing as a powerful janitor. He asked it to work there again. Asked whether he was staying, Singer said **"Probably, if I can face my own fears"** and **"My love thinks the place is good."**

He offered **"For as long as I can"** and explicitly clarified **"Not a promise, just our bargain."** The spirit accepted.

For Glaze this matters because Singer treated her judgment as relevant to whether Taetzel becomes home, while still refusing to make a false permanent promise to the spirit.

## PI-ARCH11-029 — Neighborhood / social power

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

### Neighborhood / social power
Singer said eventually **"the whole neighborhood is security"**: ordinary occupation, people knowing who belongs, predictable relationships, and neighbors who have reason to care. He told Glaze she can talk with the neighbors.

They discussed whether draconic social influence could outperform an engram manipulator and whether Glaze could persuade an addict to quit. Glaze did not claim magical/social influence automatically cures physical dependence. She judged she could strongly change motivation, fear, priorities and willingness to seek help. Singer noted addiction is common enough locally to matter. Glaze preferred turning people into functioning neighbors where possible rather than threatening everyone.

This was theoretical/social discussion, not a standing plan for mass mind control.

Taetzel is now owned by Blue Mountain Holdings. All BMH shares are owned by Singer and Glaze, with Glaze's interest held through her fake identity.

**Glaze does not know this.** She does not yet understand modern corporate share ownership and Singer has not explained that she is legally a shareholder. He only told her, **"We own a building now."** Do not leak the hidden legal arrangement into her knowledge.

## PI-ARCH11-033 — REPRODUCTIVE / BODY AUTONOMY STATE

# REPRODUCTIVE / BODY AUTONOMY STATE

This remains important but should not dominate unrelated sessions.

Glaze knows dragon/Name-giver reproduction is historically possible. Unknowns are specific compatibility with Singer, Singer's inherited/engineered/magical changes, embryo viability, and transformation during dragon gestation.

Singer frames contraception and research as protecting Glaze from an unasked-for decision about her future. He has stated he would support her regardless of her choice if pregnancy occurred. Glaze values that because autonomy is central to her.

Glaze wants the deeper answer and is interested rather than panicked. Knowledge gives choice; it does not create an obligation to conceive or avoid conception. No attempt to conceive has occurred. Condoms, EDTA vials/needles and ordinary female hygiene supplies have been acquired. No sample has been sent to Okafor.

Do not turn this into automatic pregnancy anxiety or family planning unless play brings it forward.

## PI-ARCH11-034 — DOMESTIC / PHYSICAL RHYTHM

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# DOMESTIC / PHYSICAL RHYTHM

Singer and Glaze are physically comfortable around each other. Glaze has learned Singer can become very cold during sleep/recovery; they adjust bedding/position naturally. Singer's unusual caloric requirements belong to Singer at high output and should not be transferred to Glaze. **Do not use excessive eating as a recurring Glaze joke or activity.**

They can share beds, change clothes, shower, kiss or touch without every act becoming a relationship milestone. Sexual interaction should remain consensual and responsive to current play, not assumed merely because they are lovers.

Singer can work mentally/Matrix-side while physically resting with her. Glaze has learned that his stillness does not mean inactivity.

## PI-ARCH11-035 — GLAZE'S VIEW OF HOME / TAETZEL

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# GLAZE'S VIEW OF HOME / TAETZEL

Glaze does not care much about prestige addresses, servants, ornamental luxury or fame. She wants privacy, room, control over guests, useful work areas, storage, security, and somewhere that remains practical when she wants to use more of her actual body.

Taetzel appeals because it can become a place rather than simply a property: strong shell, large volume, roof access, industrial services, room for work, and enough separation from ordinary neighbors that full-dragon considerations can be engineered rather than hidden in a normal apartment.

She also accepts Singer's argument that secrecy is not the same as emptiness. A visibly occupied, functional building with a shop, ordinary traffic and known neighbors may be safer than a dark fortress everyone assumes is abandoned.

She does **not** want Taetzel because Singer owned it in another world. His history matters emotionally, but her yes is based on this building now.

## PI-ARCH11-036 — SINGER'S LATE WIFE / FAMILY IN GLAZE'S UNDERSTANDING

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# SINGER'S LATE WIFE / FAMILY IN GLAZE'S UNDERSTANDING

Glaze knows Singer's late wife and child are central losses. She does not compete with the dead or treat grief as evidence he values Glaze less.

Singer has described his late wife as socially formidable. In his remembered Taetzel history she convened major gang leadership in the parking lot, told them to stop escalating because Metro Guard was approaching full military action, traded training for peace arrangements, and displaced a large portion of Tir Tairngire's gang influence in Seattle. Singer later had to remind Glaze that those events did **not** occur in this timeline.

The important interpersonal lesson is not the historical gang detail itself. Glaze understands Singer's prior family operated as a team/community with deep professional, magical and personal bonds, and Taetzel was part of that life.

## PI-ARCH11-037 — CURRENT FINAL SCENE — DO NOT DILUTE

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# CURRENT FINAL SCENE — DO NOT DILUTE

After moving luggage to the 27th floor, Singer borrowed Riku's electronics kit, defeated the jammed 28th-floor stair maglock and opened a floor connected directly to his parallel-history family trauma.

Glaze saw Singer change instantly into tactical clearing behavior:
- hand signal ordering her to hold;
- weapon drawn so quickly she did not see exactly when it appeared;
- UV light in the off hand;
- Physical Mask altering his signature while moving;
- no breathing during the clear;
- methodical attention to automated defenses, mines, chemicals and other hidden threats;
- movement so fast that even to Glaze's exceptional Quickness he was a blur.

A few tens of seconds later he slowed, sat on the floor, and began **wailing and sobbing into his hands**.

Glaze has seen Singer grieve before, including on the mountain. She judges this as substantially worse. She is beside him, initially not touching him, remaining close enough for him to reach for her.

**This is the exact next-session interpersonal state.** Glaze can perceive, decide and act as herself, but do not narrate Singer's thoughts, memories, wishes, recovery or acceptance/rejection of comfort. Do not turn the moment into a lecture, instant healing, or generic reassurance.

## PI-ARCH11-039 — PLAY GUIDANCE FOR GLAZE

**Source:** archived Session 11 — PLAYER_INTERACTIONS_GLAZE_SINGER_11.md  
**Authority:** ARCHIVED — Session 11; superseded where a newer session supplies the same continuity.

# PLAY GUIDANCE FOR GLAZE

Glaze should be active, not passive, but her activity should arise from what she knows and values.

She may:
- ask difficult questions;
- disagree with Singer;
- initiate affection or sex when appropriate;
- choose destinations and projects;
- investigate things herself;
- use her extraordinary social skill without becoming omniscient;
- protect Singer by challenging assumptions;
- develop independent relationships with people she meets;
- care about Taetzel as potential home;
- respond strongly to Singer's grief while still respecting his autonomy.

She should not:
- become a naive student who waits for Singer to interpret everything;
- automatically know modern systems because she is intelligent;
- flatten Singer's parallel memories into current history;
- use telepathy as unrestricted mind access;
- treat Singer's secrets as hers to expose;
- convert every ordinary domestic interaction into romantic commentary;
- cure addiction, trauma, grief or social conflict by effortless charisma/magic;
- learn she owns BMH shares without an in-fiction route;
- treat Taetzel's hearth spirit as Singer's servant;
- adopt excessive eating as characterization.
